D1 (Fri Jan 1) The Tower and the Time Capsule
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Keep tape rolls, lolly sticks and walk treasure out of Kreston's reach the moment you stop watching him. Build the tower away from where he is sitting. Azlyn can step back when a cardboard tower comes down and Kreston cannot move out of the way. Washable non-toxic paint only.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sorting the Build Box] **🧰 You need:** the recycling pile · 3 paper bags or empty boxes · masking tape.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sorting the Build Box] 1. Tip the pile into the middle of the floor and sit down in it with her.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sorting the Build Box] 2. Give her three bags and three words: **boxes**, **tubes**, **cups**. Let her sort. She will argue about the odd ones, so let the argument run.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sorting the Build Box] 3. Ask which shape she thinks will hold the most weight. Do not answer. Tell her you will find out at 9:15 and leave the question hanging.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands] 1. Build a tower as tall as you can with no tape. Let her place every piece. Do not correct the wobbly ones.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands] 2. It will fall. Say *what could we change?* instead of *that was too narrow,* and wait for her answer even when it takes a while.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands] 3. Build again, this time widest boxes at the bottom. Ask her which is stronger and let her push it to find out.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands] 4. Measure the tallest one against her. Mark the height on the wall with a pencil and write **TOWER, JAN 1** beside it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands] 5. Now hand her the tape and let her build a third one however she likes. Leave her to discover what tape lets her get away with.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tallest Tower That Stands] 6. Leave the best tower standing. She will come back to it at 4:15.
    [🌳 Get Outside: First Walk of the Year] Bundle up and walk the route you will walk all year. Look at the trees today,
    [🌳 Get Outside: First Walk of the Year] bare and sleeping, all structure and no leaves, the same thing she has been
    [🌳 Get Outside: First Walk of the Year] stacking all morning. Hand her a paper bag and one instruction: find one thing
    [🌳 Get Outside: First Walk of the Year] worth keeping. It goes in the capsule this afternoon, so tell her that now.
    [🌳 Get Outside: First Walk of the Year] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] Morning was tall, loud and falling over. This afternoon you seal a box and put
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] it away for a year.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] 1. Paint her palm with the brush rather than dipping her hand. Press flat, count to three, lift straight up. Write **January** and **3** beside it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] 2. Do Kreston's on the same sheet while he is calm, flannel already in your hand.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] 3. Stand them both against the door frame. Mark, date and write the names. Draw round Azlyn's flat hand next to her mark. Do everyone in the house so she can see the row.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] 4. Ask her four questions and write the answers **word for word**: favourite colour, favourite food, favourite song, what she wants to do this year. Do not tidy her grammar. Her wrong words are the ones you will want to read next December.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] 5. Let her draw the family. Whatever comes out goes in.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] 6. Load the box: the drawing, her answers, the walk treasure, a photo. Photograph everything first. Boxes go missing, and the photos on your phone will still be there.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Time Capsule] 7. Tape it shut, write **OPEN DECEMBER 31, 2027** across the lid, and put it somewhere she can see and not reach.
D2 (Sat Jan 2) The Box House
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The craft knife is adult-only. Put it back out of reach between cuts, on a shelf and not the arm of the sofa. Check inside the box for staples before either child gets in. Never leave Kreston in the box house alone, and keep the door flap open the whole time he is in it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Box] **🧰 You need:** the big box · masking tape · 1 permanent marker.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Box] 1. Let her climb in before anything else happens. She will do this anyway.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Box] 2. Ask what it should be: house, shop, boat, rocket. Write her answer on the side in marker. The decision is now fixed and it is hers.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Box] 3. Give her the tape and let her mark where the door and the windows go. She decides now, and you cut along her marks at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Box House] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Box House] 1. Cut the door on three sides so it swings open. Do this yourself with her standing back, and say why she is standing back.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Box House] 2. Cut two windows where she taped. Follow her marks even when they are in daft places.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Box House] 3. Hand her the offcuts and the tape and let her build a roof. It will be crooked and it will hold.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Box House] 4. Give her the marker. Every surface, inside and out, is hers to draw on.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Box House] 5. Write a house number on the front and let her choose the number.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Box House] 6. Get in with her. Sit down on the floor inside it and let her show you round.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Shape Hunt] Walk the street looking for the shapes she sorted yesterday. Windows are
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Shape Hunt] rectangles, drain covers are circles, road signs are triangles, gates are made
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Shape Hunt] of squares. Ask her to find you three circles before you turn back. Say so out
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Shape Hunt] loud when she finds one you didn't see.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Shape Hunt] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] She moves somebody in this afternoon. Small boxes, cups and tape turn into
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] the furniture.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] 1. Ask what a house needs. Take her list at face value even when it is a bed, a cake and a dog.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] 2. Make the bed first: a shoebox with the fabric folded into it. Let her decide where it goes.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] 3. Turn a tall box on its end for a table, cups upside down for stools.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] 4. Cut a rectangle of card and tape it inside as a picture. She draws whatever hangs in it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] 5. Make one thing from her list that you would not have thought of. Follow her design down to the parts you would fix.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] 6. Move a doll or a teddy in. Give it a name and write the name on the door.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Furnishing the Box House] 7. Turn the big light off and give her the torch. The cardboard glows and she will not want to come out.
D3 (Sun Jan 3) Ramps and Bridges
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Dried beans and pasta are a choking hazard and they will end up on the floor. Sweep before you put Kreston down, and keep the catching box off the floor when he is loose in the room. Scissors stay with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Taping the Tubes] **🧰 You need:** cardboard tubes · masking tape · scissors · a smooth wall or the side of a wardrobe.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Taping the Tubes] 1. Cut two or three tubes in half lengthways so they become open channels. You cut, she holds.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Taping the Tubes] 2. Tape one channel to the wall at a slope. Roll a dried bean down it and watch where it lands.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Taping the Tubes] 3. Ask her where the next one should go so the bean keeps travelling. Tape it exactly where she says, even if it will not work.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run] 1. Put the catching box on the floor at the bottom. You aim the whole run at that box.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run] 2. Work upward. She chooses each next position, you tape it. Narrate as you go: *I'm taping this one steeper, let's see.*
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run] 3. Send one bean down the whole run after each piece you add.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run] 4. It will fail somewhere. Look at where the bean stopped, change that one joint and nothing else, then test again.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run] 5. Once beans run the whole way, try pasta, then the ball. Each weight runs the course in its own way, and she will spot that before you say anything.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ball Run] 6. Leave it taped up. You take it down tomorrow as part of the demolition.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Bridges] Find a puddle, a gutter or a gap between two stones and build a bridge across
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Bridges] it with sticks. Test each one with a pebble. Most of them fall in. Ask her
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Bridges] what a longer stick does, then what two sticks laid side by side do. The
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Bridges] gloves come home wet.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Bridges] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] The bridge goes between two chairs and has to hold a teddy while the gap keeps
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] growing. Cardboard gets strong once she changes its shape.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] 1. Set the chairs close together. Lay one flat piece of card between them and stand teddy on it. It holds.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] 2. Move the chairs a hand's width apart and try again. Keep going until it sags.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] 3. Ask *what could we change?* and wait. Odds are she says a bigger piece of card. Try it, and watch it sag again.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] 4. Show her one trick and then step back: fold the card into a concertina, or roll it into tubes and lay them side by side. Let her work out which way up it goes.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] 5. Test with teddy, then the tin of beans, then both at once.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] 6. Push the chairs as far apart as the bridge will survive and mark the floor with tape.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bridge That Holds Teddy] 7. Take a photo of the winning bridge with teddy standing on it.
D4 (Mon Jan 4) Demolition Day
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Tie the string to something that cannot come down on top of her: a door frame, not a curtain pole or a shelf. Take the sock down and put the string away the moment you finish. A hanging loop of string is a strangulation risk, so do not leave it up overnight or within reach of either child.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cup Pyramids] **🧰 You need:** paper cups, 20 or so · a flat bit of floor.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cup Pyramids] 1. Build a pyramid together: six cups along the bottom, then five, then four.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cup Pyramids] 2. Take one cup out of the bottom row and see what happens. Then put it back and take one from the top instead.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cup Pyramids] 3. Ask which cup is doing the most work. Take her answer as it comes and tell her you will test it for real at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment] 1. Build three towers side by side, all the same height. Tower one has a wide base. Tower two is a straight stack. Tower three is widest at the top on purpose.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment] 2. Give each one a number and write the numbers on paper.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment] 3. Push the floor near tower one with a flat hand. Count how many pushes before it goes. Write the number down. Repeat for two and three.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment] 4. Ask her which fell first and which held longest, and write her explanation word for word next to the numbers.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment] 5. Rebuild the winner and let her push it over herself, as hard as she likes.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Knock-Down Experiment] 6. Now take down the ball run together and add its tubes to the pile.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stone Stacking] Find flat stones and stack them as high as they will go on a wall, a step or a
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stone Stacking] tree stump. She learned the wide-base rule indoors and will use it out here
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stone Stacking] before you mention it. Leave the stack standing for the next person and walk
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stone Stacking] past it tomorrow to see whether it survived.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stone Stacking] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] This morning she knocked the towers over with her own hands. This afternoon
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] she pulls a weight back, lets go, and the swing does the work for her.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] 1. Fill the sock a third full, knot it, and tie the string tight around the knot.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] 2. Hang the free end from the door frame so the sock swings a hand's width above the floor.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] 3. Build a tower right under the swing. Pull the sock back and let go. Do not throw it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] 4. Rebuild in the same spot and try releasing from lower down. Ask her which knocked more over.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] 5. Move the tower further away and let her work out where the swing stops reaching.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] 6. Let her build a tower she thinks can survive it. Test it. Let her be right or wrong without commentary.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Wrecking Ball] 7. Finish by building one tower together that you both agree is too good to knock down, then knock it down anyway.
D5 (Tue Jan 5) Bricks and Mortar
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The oven is on for three hours today, so say it out loud when she comes near, and keep Kreston out of the kitchen while the trays move. Salt dough is dangerous if eaten, and seriously so for a 7-month-old and for any dog in the house. Count the bricks in and out and store them high.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Bricks] **🧰 You need:** the salt dough · a rolling pin · a table knife · a floured board.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Bricks] 1. Give her a fist-sized lump and let her squash it for a few minutes before anything useful happens.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Bricks] 2. Let her roll it out to about a finger's thickness and leave the uneven patches where they are.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Bricks] 3. Cut rough rectangles with the table knife. They do not need to match. Aim for thirty and stop when she stops.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks] 1. Lay the cut bricks on the lined tray without touching each other.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks] 2. Let her press a mark into each one with her thumb, or scratch a line with the pencil. She owns the ones she marks.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks] 3. Set them in the oven at its lowest setting. They need two to three hours, so put them in now and take them out around lunch.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks] 4. While they bake, knead the leftover dough together. Give her a big lump, take one yourself, and sit with it. Ten minutes of quiet kneading does more for a wound-up morning than any instruction.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks] 5. Make three or four larger pieces (a doorstep, an arch, a chimney) and add them to a second tray.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Salt Dough Bricks] 6. When they come out, spread them on a cold tray and leave them until 1:00, because warm bricks crumble.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Cold Walk] Keep it short and brisk today, because the oven is on. Go past yesterday's
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Cold Walk] stone stack first and see whether it survived. Look for frost on car
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Cold Walk] roofs and in the shade at the bottom of walls. Let her breathe out and watch
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Cold Walk] it. Find one brick wall and put her hand flat on it so she can feel how cold
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Cold Walk] stone gets and see how many bricks it took.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Cold Walk] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall] The wall goes up on a tray, one row at a time. She lays every brick herself,
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall] and the thirty she cut are the whole supply.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall] 1. Lay a row of bricks in a straight line along the tray. Push them tight together.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall] 2. Build a second row straight on top with every joint matching. Push it and watch the whole wall hinge apart.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall] 3. Rebuild the second row offset, each brick bridging the gap below it. Push again. This one holds, and she will feel the difference in her hand.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall] 4. Keep going up. Use the paste between rows like mortar if the wall gets tall enough to need it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall] 5. Leave a gap for a door and lay one long brick across the top of it. That is an arch, and it is holding up everything above it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Building the Brick Wall] 6. Build a second wall at a right angle to the first so they lean on each other.
D6 (Wed Jan 6) Triangles Hold
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Take the zip line down and put the string away the second you finish. String at toddler height is a strangulation risk, and a serious one. Loose lolly sticks stay off the floor while Kreston is down. They splinter, and they fit down his throat. He gets a taped structure too big for his mouth and nothing looser than that, and it comes back to you the moment you stop watching. The scissors stay with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Lolly Stick Shapes] **🧰 You need:** lolly sticks · masking tape cut into short tabs.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Lolly Stick Shapes] 1. Tape four sticks into a square. Let her do the taping, however it comes out, with your thumb holding the corners.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Lolly Stick Shapes] 2. Tape three sticks into a triangle beside it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Lolly Stick Shapes] 3. Press one finger on the top corner of each. The square folds flat and the triangle holds. Say nothing else about it and leave both on the table until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Triangle Test] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Triangle Test] 1. Hand her the folded square and ask her to make it stop folding. Then wait, and hold the silence a good while longer than feels comfortable.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Triangle Test] 2. If she gets stuck, put one loose stick on the table beside it and say nothing more. Most children lay it across the diagonal within a minute.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Triangle Test] 3. Once the square has a diagonal, push it together again. It holds now, because the diagonal turned it into two triangles.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Triangle Test] 4. Build a tall tower out of taped triangles. Build a second out of taped squares. Race them upward.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Triangle Test] 5. Load both with the toy and push both over. Write down which survived.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Triangle Test] 6. Go and look at the box house from Saturday and find where a triangle would fix its saggy roof. Add one.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Stick Shelter] Lean long sticks against a fence, a low branch or a wall to make a tent shape,
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Stick Shelter] then let go and see whether it stands. Push the top the way she pushed the one
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Stick Shelter] on the table. A triangle out here behaves the same as one indoors. Put a stone
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Stick Shelter] or a leaf inside for her to shelter, and come back tomorrow to see whether the
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Stick Shelter] wind took it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Stick Shelter] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line] She has spent the week building things that push down. A zip line pulls, the
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line] opposite force and a new idea for her.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line] 1. Tie the string tight between the chair backs, one end higher than the other. She decides which end is higher.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line] 2. Tape the paper cup to the cardboard ring so the cup hangs underneath, then thread the string through the ring before you tie the second end.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line] 3. Load the cup with a small toy at the high end and let go.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line] 4. Once it stops halfway, ask what would make it keep going. Raise the high end and try again. Let her be the one who lifts it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line] 5. Race two different weights down the same line, and have her call the winner out loud before she lets go.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Zip Line] 6. Slacken the string right off and try again so she sees a sagging line stop the cup dead.
D7 (Thu Jan 7) The Fort
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Nothing heavy on top of the fort and nothing that can fall inward. Books go on the outside edges, never on the roof. No cords, no fairy lights, no string left from yesterday. Never leave Kreston in there on his own, and keep pillows and loose blankets away from his face.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Fort Spot] **🧰 You need:** the blanket pile · masking tape · her.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Fort Spot] 1. Walk her round the downstairs and ask where the fort should go. Take the answer seriously even if it is the hallway.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Fort Spot] 2. Test it together. Can two people sit up in it, is there something to hang a blanket from, will the door still open.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing the Fort Spot] 3. Mark the corners on the floor with tape. That is the footprint, and the fort you build at 9:15 stays inside it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Blanket Fort] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Blanket Fort] 1. Set the chairs on the tape marks with their backs outward. That is the frame.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Blanket Fort] 2. Drape the first sheet across the top and clip it to the chair backs. Clips hold where tucked edges pull loose.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Blanket Fort] 3. Ask her where the door goes. Peg that edge so it can be lifted and dropped.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Blanket Fort] 4. Send her inside with the cushions to build the floor. Leave her to it for a full ten minutes.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Blanket Fort] 5. Once the roof sags, remind her what fixed the sagging bridge on Sunday and let her work out the rest.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Blanket Fort] 6. Weight every outside edge with books and line the doorway with the salt dough bricks, so she walks past Tuesday's work on her way in.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Outdoor Den] Do the same thing outside with whatever is there, whether that is a bench and a
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Outdoor Den] blanket, sticks against a fence, or a sheet over the washing line. Check
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Outdoor Den] Wednesday's stick shelter on the way past and see whether the wind took it. Get
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Outdoor Den] her to sit inside the outdoor den for a minute in the cold, then come in to
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Outdoor Den] the warm one.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Outdoor Den] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort] The whole afternoon happens inside the fort. She eats in there, reads in
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort] there, draws in there, and it stays up until bedtime.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort] 1. Move in. Floor covering first, then the torch, then the books.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort] 2. Eat the afternoon snack inside. Food in a fort is most of the appeal.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort] 3. Turn the big light off and do shadow shapes on the roof (triangle, square, house).
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort] 4. Read two books in there by torchlight, and let her hold the torch even though it will point at the ceiling the whole time.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort] 5. Look back through the week's photos together: the tower, the box house, the bridge with teddy on it, the brick wall. Ask which one she would build again.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Living in the Fort] 6. Let her draw the fort from the inside while she is inside it.
D8 (Fri Jan 8) Shakers and Spoons
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Dried rice, lentils and pasta on the floor are a choking hazard for a 7-month-old, and a taped lid can still come off. Tape all the way round each pot, check every seal before it goes down, and keep the small pots away from Kreston.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Listening to the Cupboard] **🧰 You need:** the dry-goods cupboard, open · 1 metal spoon · 5 empty pots with lids.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Listening to the Cupboard] 1. Sit her in front of the open cupboard and let her tap the packets with the back of the spoon. Rice, pasta, lentils, cereal, sugar. Each answers in a different voice.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Listening to the Cupboard] 2. Shut your eyes and let her tap one for you to name. Get one wrong on purpose.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Listening to the Cupboard] 3. Line up the five pots and let her decide which packet goes in which. You label them at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set] 1. Roll the paper into a cone, tape the seam and stand it in the first pot. She pours, you hold. Some goes on the table and that is the price.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set] 2. Fill each pot a third full. A pot packed to the lid makes almost no sound, so let her overfill one and hear it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set] 3. Tape every lid all the way round twice and press the seams flat. Write the contents on the side of each pot in marker.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set] 4. Shake them one at a time with her eyes shut. Ask her to point at the loudest before she opens them.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set] 5. Stand all five along the table, quietest to loudest. Move them as often as she wants.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shaker Set] 6. Number the bases one to five once she settles. She comes back to this line at 4:15.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Tapping the Railings] Take a wooden spoon out with you and tap what you pass. Railings ring and hold
    [🌳 Get Outside: Tapping the Railings] the note, fence posts thud and stop dead, a wheelie bin booms, a brick wall
    [🌳 Get Outside: Tapping the Railings] gives back nothing. January metal is cold enough that she will want gloves on
    [🌳 Get Outside: Tapping the Railings] and then off again to feel it. Let her find the loudest thing on the street and
    [🌳 Get Outside: Tapping the Railings] stay at it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Tapping the Railings] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song] The shakers have their order. This afternoon Azlyn plays one rhyme four ways
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song] and decides which way comes next.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song] 1. Sit everyone in a ring, shakers in the middle, saucepan by your knee, scarf in Azlyn's hand.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song] 2. Pick a rhyme the whole house knows. Twinkle, Twinkle or The Wheels on the Bus. Sing it through once with a shaker each.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song] 3. Loud version. Saucepan on the beat, cardboard tube as a megaphone, pasta and lentils flat out. One verse, and one verse alone.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song] 4. Quiet version. Sugar shaker on its own, everyone whispering, heads close together. She drops her voice to match yours.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song] 5. Fast, then slow. Slow is the hard one, and one verse at half speed lasts longer than either of you expects.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Loud Song, Quiet Song] 6. Hand her the scarf. Scarf up is loud, scarf down is quiet, and she runs the band. Follow her even when she flips it every two words.
D9 (Sat Jan 9) Humpty Dumpty's Wall
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Shell fragments have sharp edges and are exactly the size Kreston puts in his mouth. Keep the tray and the glued sheet up high, sweep the floor before he goes down, and wipe egg off a hard floor straight away because it is slippery.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on the Eggs] **🧰 You need:** 4 cold boiled eggs · 1 permanent marker · 1 egg cup.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on the Eggs] 1. Hand her the marker and one egg at a time. Faces, hats, whatever she wants. Marker takes a minute to dry on a shell, so hold each one by the ends.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on the Eggs] 2. Say the rhyme together while she draws. She will reach the line about nobody putting him together again and look up at you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on the Eggs] 3. Stand the best one in the egg cup on the windowsill. Nobody touches him before 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record] 1. Line the tray with the folded towel and set it on the kitchen floor. She stacks the books into a wall beside it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record] 2. Sit Humpty on top and say the rhyme once with him up there.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record] 3. First drop from ankle height, straight into the tray. Measure with the tape and write the number down before she opens her hand.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record] 4. Ask her to call it before every drop: cracked or not cracked. Write her answer beside the number. Knee height next, then hip.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record] 5. Take the towel out for the last egg and drop it on the bare tray from ankle height. It breaks where it was safe five minutes ago.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg Drop Record] 6. Read the paper back to her. Her numbers, her guesses, and the one that caught her out.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Low Wall Balance] Find a low garden wall, a kerb edge or the flat top of a step and walk her
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Low Wall Balance] along it holding one hand. She sits at the far end with her legs over and says
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Low Wall Balance] the rhyme from up there, then jumps into your hands. January stone keeps its
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Low Wall Balance] frost all morning in the shade, so put your own hand flat on the top first.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Low Wall Balance] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] Humpty's shell is still sitting on the tray. She glues him back together this
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] afternoon, the one thing the rhyme insists nobody could do.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] 1. Draw a big egg outline on the card in pencil, or let her draw it and go over her line so she can see it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] 2. Tip the shell onto a sheet of paper and let her press it small with the back of a spoon. Fingers work too.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] 3. Mix the paste if you are using it: two tablespoons of flour, water stirred in until it runs like cream.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] 4. Cover a small patch of the egg at a time. Paste the whole outline at once and it dries before she has filled a third of it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] 5. She presses the shell down. Let her go outside the line and leave gaps, and keep your hands off.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] 6. Crayon in the rest: the wall underneath, the king's horses, a face on the shell.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Mended Egg] 7. Write her title along the bottom in her own words, then put it up high to dry.
D10 (Sun Jan 10) Five Little Ducks
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** January pond water is cold enough to matter and the edge is muddy and steep. Hold her hand at the water, throw the food from well back, and stay between both children and the bank. Geese take fingers with the bread. The pegs and the small rhyme-bag objects are choke-sized, so they come off the floor before Kreston does.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Making the Five Ducks] **🧰 You need:** 1 flattened cereal box · scissors (adult only) · crayons · 5 clothes pegs.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Making the Five Ducks] 1. Draw five duck shapes on the plain side of the box. Rough ovals with a beak on the front will do.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Making the Five Ducks] 2. You cut, she colours. Give each one something different, and let her choose which is the mother duck.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Making the Five Ducks] 3. Clip a peg to the bottom of each so they stand up. Line all five along the table and count them twice.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over the Hill and Far Away] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over the Hill and Far Away] 1. Turn the laundry basket upside down in the middle of the floor for the hill. Spread the tea towel in front of it for the pond and tape the corners flat.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over the Hill and Far Away] 2. Stand all five ducks on the pond. Run a strip of masking tape across the floor behind the hill to mark where far away starts.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over the Hill and Far Away] 3. Sing the first verse. On the line about only four coming back, she takes one duck over the hill and stands it on the tape.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over the Hill and Far Away] 4. Count what is left on the pond after each verse, touching each duck as its number comes. She does the quacking, all of it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over the Hill and Far Away] 5. Keep going down to none, and sit in the quiet of an empty pond before the last verse.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over the Hill and Far Away] 6. Mother duck calls and all five come back over together. She carries them, and she will want the whole thing again, so do it again.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking in a Duck Line] Walk the block in single file. She goes first as the mother duck, everyone
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking in a Duck Line] behind her waddles, and at each corner the person at the back moves to the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking in a Duck Line] front. Count off before you set out and count again at home to see whether
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking in a Duck Line] anybody went over the hill and far away. January puddles have ice at the rim
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking in a Duck Line] and she will stamp every one of them.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking in a Duck Line] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag] One object per rhyme goes into a pillowcase, and the bag decides what the house
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag] sings.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag] 1. Gather the objects and give each one its rhyme out loud. The spoon ran away with the dish, the mouse ran up the watch, the sock is Diddle Diddle Dumpling.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag] 2. Draw a picture label on card for each one, rough and fast, and lay it beside its object.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag] 3. Everything into the pillowcase. She reaches in without looking, feels about, pulls one out.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag] 4. Whatever comes out, the whole house sings it. No skipping and no swapping.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag] 5. Let her guess by feel before she looks, one guess out loud.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rhyme Bag] 6. Once the bag is empty, lay the objects in the order they arrived and sing the set through as one medley.
D11 (Mon Jan 11) The Beat in Every Word
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A saucepan banged at close range is loud enough to hurt a 7-month-old's ears. Keep Kreston an arm's length back from the kit, give him his own quiet tub, and stop the session before it turns into hitting for volume. Wooden spoons swing at exactly his head height.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Spoon and the Saucepan] **🧰 You need:** 1 saucepan · 1 wooden spoon · everybody's name.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Spoon and the Saucepan] 1. Turn the pan upside down on the floor between you and give her the spoon.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Spoon and the Saucepan] 2. Say her name in pieces and hit the pan once for each piece. AZ-LYN, two hits. KRES-TON, two. BROOK-LYN, two.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Spoon and the Saucepan] 3. Let her do yours, then let her do words that are not names. Hit whatever she says, however many pieces she reckons it has.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Drum Kit] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Drum Kit] 1. Line the tin, the tub, the cereal box and the saucepan along the floor and tape their bases down so they stop travelling.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Drum Kit] 2. Hit each one and let her say which is high and which is low. Stuff the tea towel into the box, hit it again, and she hears the same drum go dull.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Drum Kit] 3. Names first, one hit per piece. Everyone in the house, then the neighbours, then the cat, then people she has invented.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Drum Kit] 4. Move on to food for longer words. BREAD is one hit. BA-NA-NA is three. SPA-GHET-TI is four and she will want that one over and over.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Drum Kit] 5. Play a rhyme line by line. TWIN-KLE TWIN-KLE LIT-TLE STAR, one hit per piece. Number the drums one to four in marker if she starts calling for a particular one.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Drum Kit] 6. She plays something short and you copy it back, then you play and she copies. Keep them to three hits.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping Out the Names] Walk the street and stamp what you pass. GATE is one stamp. LAMP-POST is two.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping Out the Names] LET-TER-BOX is three. Frozen puddles sit in the shade all day this month and a
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping Out the Names] stamp on thin ice gives her a crack and a splinter pattern. She names the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping Out the Names] thing, she stamps it, and you take her count.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping Out the Names] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] She lays a row of objects along the floor, gives each one a sound, and plays
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] the row left to right.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] 1. Run the masking tape in a long line down the floor, or lay the paper strip out. That is the row.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] 2. Start it off yourself. Spoon, cup, spoon, cup, spoon, cup, saying each one aloud. Ask her what comes next and wait through the silence.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] 3. Give every object a sound. Spoon is a tap on the tin, cup is a slap on the tub, sock is silence. She will fill the row with socks.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] 4. Play the row left to right, pointing at each object as its sound comes. Then again, faster. Then again with her pointing and you playing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] 5. Hand the row over. She builds the next one and you play it back, note for note, including the row that is seven socks and a spoon.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] 6. Take one object out of the middle of a repeating row and play it again. Ask her what went missing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pattern You Can Hear] 7. Photograph the last row before you clear it up, then let her play it from the photo.
D12 (Tue Jan 12) Twinkle, Twinkle
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The skewer is adult-only and goes back in the drawer between cups, not on the table. If the torch takes button cells, tape the compartment shut; a swallowed button battery burns through a child's throat within hours. At the pool, Kreston stays in arm's reach in the water, every second.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Finding the Darkest Corner] **🧰 You need:** 1 torch · every door in the house · her.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Finding the Darkest Corner] 1. Walk her round with the torch off, shutting doors and pulling curtains as you go. Stand in each room for a slow count of ten and let your eyes adjust.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Finding the Darkest Corner] 2. Ask her which one is darkest. Bathrooms and the cupboard under the stairs tend to win.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Finding the Darkest Corner] 3. Test the winner. Shine the torch at the ceiling and watch the circle arrive. Leave those curtains shut for the rest of the day.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Star Cup] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Star Cup] 1. Sit her at the table with a cup and the marker and let her dot a pattern over the base and the sides. Ten or fifteen dots is plenty. A hundred dots makes a lamp.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Star Cup] 2. Stand the cup on the old magazine and push the skewer through each dot. You do all the pushing, she counts them off as they go.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Star Cup] 3. Make a second cup with a different pattern and let her name them both.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Star Cup] 4. Take the cups and the torch into the dark room. Torch pointing up, cup over the end of it, a strip of tape round the join if the fit is loose.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Star Cup] 5. Light off. Her stars land on the ceiling and travel when you tilt the torch. Lie on your backs underneath and sing the rhyme.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Star Cup] 6. Hand her the torch. It will go across the wall, the floor and your face.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Sleeping Street] Walk the usual route hunting for things that have shut down for winter. Buds
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Sleeping Street] on the bare twigs are sealed tight and will sit there until March. Ask her
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Sleeping Street] where the birds go at night, take her answer at face value, then go and look at the
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Sleeping Street] places she might be right about. Dark comes before half four this month.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Sleeping Street] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] Twinkle, Twinkle carries a tune the whole house already owns, so the words can
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] be about anything. Azlyn picks what goes in the verse, and she picks her
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] brother.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] 1. Ask Azlyn what Kreston did today. Write it down as she says it, including the parts that did not happen.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] 2. Pick four items off the list. The daft ones survive, and she does the choosing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] 3. Fit them to the tune a line at a time, singing each line the moment you have it so she hears whether it lands. Too many pieces crammed into a line is the usual trouble.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] 4. Sing the finished verse twice so she has it in her. Then let her change a word.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] 5. Record it on your phone, her singing and you underneath her. One take. Do not ask her for a tidier version.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Own Song] 6. She draws the picture while the recording plays back. Kreston's blanket goes over her lap, or over him.
D13 (Wed Jan 13) Ten in the Bed
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Ten in the Bed ends with somebody going over the edge. Do it on a mattress on the floor, or pillow the drop side and stay on it. Card coins only; real coins are the classic swallowed object at this age, and Kreston will be under the table hunting for whatever rolled.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Lining Up the Bears] **🧰 You need:** 10 soft toys · 1 blanket · a stretch of floor.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Lining Up the Bears] 1. Tip the basket out and let her line all ten along the edge of the blanket, in whatever order she wants.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Lining Up the Bears] 2. Count the row together, touching each one as the number comes out. Let her tell you their names and write them down.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Lining Up the Bears] 3. Ask her who is going over the edge first. Whoever she picks goes on the end until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Roll Over, Roll Over] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Roll Over, Roll Over] 1. Put the mattress or the duvet on the floor, or use the bed with both pillows packed along the open side. Everybody goes over an edge this morning, so the edge wants to be low.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Roll Over, Roll Over] 2. Lay the ten toys along the blanket in the order she made, with her at one end of the row.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Roll Over, Roll Over] 3. Sing the verse. On "roll over" everyone rolls, toys included, and the one on the end goes off the side.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Roll Over, Roll Over] 4. Count who is left after every verse, touching each one, then write the new number on the paper. Nine. Eight. Seven.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Roll Over, Roll Over] 5. Let her say the number that opens the next verse. She will skip one, so count the row again and find out who is right.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Roll Over, Roll Over] 6. The last verse has one left and nobody to roll into. Sit in that, then let her throw all ten back on and start again.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Doors to Number Ten] Walk down one side of the street reading the house numbers out loud. Find
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Doors to Number Ten] number one and walk as far as number ten, and let her spot each numeral on the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Doors to Number Ten] gate before you say it. Odd on one side and even on the other will bother her,
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Doors to Number Ten] and it is worth crossing the road to sort out.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Counting the Doors to Number Ten] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] Five Currant Buns in a Baker's Shop wants a shop, a baker and somebody with
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] money in their hand.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] 1. Cut five circles from the cereal box and ten coins from what is left. She draws the currants on, as many per bun as she likes.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] 2. Set the tray on a low table as the counter and the shoebox behind it as the till. She is the baker, and the shop opens when she says.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] 3. Count the buns onto the tray out loud. Five, touching each one.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] 4. Sing the verse, walk up with a coin, buy one bun and carry it away. She hands the bun over and puts the coin in the till herself.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] 5. After each sale, ask how many are left and let her count the tray from the start. Wait out the recount.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] 6. Sell all five, then swap. She counts the coins out of the till one at a time into your hand.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bun Shop] 7. Shut the shop. Count the coins in the till and the buns in your hand, and stack both on the tray for tomorrow.
D14 (Thu Jan 14) The Marching Band
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Build the hill low and wide, and stay within arm's reach the whole time she is standing on the top. A stack of cushions slides apart under a three-year-old's feet. Tape Kreston's shaker all the way round twice and check the seal every time before it reaches his hands. In the marching line, keep the spoons and shakers clear of his head; in the carrier it sits at swinging height.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Handing Out the Instruments] **🧰 You need:** the week's shakers, drums and spoons in one basket · a hat each.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Handing Out the Instruments] 1. Put the basket down in the middle of the floor and let her hand the instruments out. She decides who gets what, including what you get.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Handing Out the Instruments] 2. Hats on. Anything counts: a sun hat, a woolly one, a saucepan.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Handing Out the Instruments] 3. Everyone plays their own thing at once for thirty seconds, then stops dead the moment she says stop. Do that twice and leave it until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Up the Hill and Down Again] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Up the Hill and Down Again] 1. Build the hill. Four to six cushions stacked in the middle of the floor, wide enough to walk up and firm enough to stand on at the top.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Up the Hill and Down Again] 2. Tape the sheet of paper to the wooden spoon for a flag. She decides what goes on the flag and she carries it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Up the Hill and Down Again] 3. Line up behind the flag with the instruments and the hats. March round the room once to get the step going, everyone starting on the same foot.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Up the Hill and Down Again] 4. Sing the Grand Old Duke of York. Up the cushions on "up", down the other side on "down".
    [🎨 The Main Event: Up the Hill and Down Again] 5. Halfway up and halfway down, stop dead on the word and hold it. Hold it longer than feels comfortable. She will ask for it eight times.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Up the Hill and Down Again] 6. Tape Kreston's shaker twice round and check the seal, then bring him into the line for the last two runs.
    [🌳 Get Outside: In Step Round the Block] March the block. Left, right, left, right, called out loud, everyone setting
    [🌳 Get Outside: In Step Round the Block] off on the same foot. She loses the step inside twenty paces and finds it
    [🌳 Get Outside: In Step Round the Block] again. Halt at each corner and freeze for a count of five. January pavements
    [🌳 Get Outside: In Step Round the Block] are wet or gritted, so drop the calling for a stretch and listen to the boots
    [🌳 Get Outside: In Step Round the Block] instead.
    [🌳 Get Outside: In Step Round the Block] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Whole House Parade] The parade needs a route, and she draws it before anybody marches anywhere.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Whole House Parade] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Whole House Parade] 1. Spread the big sheet on the floor and draw the downstairs as one long line. Front door, hall, kitchen, living room, back to the door. She adds the rooms you forget.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Whole House Parade] 2. Mark four or five stops along the line, one per doorway. She picks a rhyme for each stop and you draw a small picture of it on the map.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Whole House Parade] 3. Tape an X on the floor at each stop so the band knows where to halt.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Whole House Parade] 4. Run it once with you leading. Play and march between the stops, halt on the X, everyone sings that doorway's rhyme.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Whole House Parade] 5. Run it again with Azlyn out front and the flag in her hand. She sets the speed, and the speed will be wrong.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Whole House Parade] 6. Third run, Kreston leads from your hip and Azlyn walks behind him with the drum.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Whole House Parade] 7. Record the last run on your phone, walking backwards in front of the band. Sit on the floor together and watch it once.
D15 (Fri Jan 15) The Made-Up Song
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A glass jar carried by a three-year-old with Kreston on the floor is a bad combination. Use a plastic tub, or keep the jar on the table and let her post the strips standing up. Loose paper goes in his mouth, so sweep before you put him down.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Who the Song Is For] **🧰 You need:** paper, 4–5 sheets · 1 pen · the kitchen table.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Who the Song Is For] 1. Ask her to name everyone who lives in this house. Write one name at the top of each sheet in big letters and let her choose which comes first.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Who the Song Is For] 2. Pick the tune. Twinkle Twinkle, Frère Jacques, Happy Birthday, anything she sings unprompted. Sing it once with the words it came with.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Who the Song Is For] 3. Ask her one true thing about the first person. Write the answer under the name. That line opens the song at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Verse for Each Person] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Verse for Each Person] 1. Sing the tune once with her name in it, so she hears the shape before she has to make one.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Verse for Each Person] 2. Hand her the spoon and take the first sheet. Say the name at the top and let her sing. Write it down word for word, wrong grammar included.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Verse for Each Person] 3. Keep a slow beat on the upturned saucepan underneath her. The beat carries her over the gap where the words run out.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Verse for Each Person] 4. Work through every sheet. Do not offer her a rhyme and do not tidy a line she has sung. Kreston's verse will be about his feet.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Verse for Each Person] 5. Read the whole thing back as one song and put her own name in the last line.
    [🎨 The Main Event: A Verse for Each Person] 6. Record one take on your phone. One. Sing it again afterwards with your hands free.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Singing Walk] Take the song out and sing it down the street. Cold January air carries a voice further than she expects, and she will hear her own noise come back off the houses. Sing your verse first so she sees a grown adult do it outdoors. Let her pick who gets a verse: a dog, a parked van, the woman at the bus stop.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Singing Walk] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Good Thing Jar] The jar lands on the kitchen table today and stays all week. Good things go in on paper strips, and on Thursday you tip the lot out and read them.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Good Thing Jar] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Good Thing Jar] 1. Give her the empty jar and the crayons and leave her to decorate it. Tape covers a lot. So do stickers.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Good Thing Jar] 2. Write **THE GOOD THING JAR** on a strip of tape and let her stick it on crooked.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Good Thing Jar] 3. Cut the paper into strips together. Twenty is plenty and thirty is better.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Good Thing Jar] 4. Walk the house room by room. In each room, name one thing somebody did for somebody else today: the tea you made, the toy Azlyn handed Kreston, the door Joseph shut.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Good Thing Jar] 5. Write each one on a strip, read it out loud, and hand it to her to post. She posts every strip.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Good Thing Jar] 6. Do three strips about her without using the word *good* or the word *girl*. Name the action: **Azlyn carried the basket to the door.**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Good Thing Jar] 7. Put the jar where she can reach it. Anybody can post one, any time, and nobody reads them until Thursday.
D16 (Sat Jan 16) The Show for One
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** She will climb on a dining chair to reach the curtain. You hang it, she stands back. Nothing tied at neck height, no cord left running across the doorway, and no draped sheet anywhere over Kreston. Take the curtain down before bed.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The One Ticket] **🧰 You need:** card or a cereal box · crayons · scissors (yours) · 1 pen.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The One Ticket] 1. Tell her a show is happening at 1:00 and one ticket exists. She decides who gets it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The One Ticket] 2. Cut a rectangle of card and hand it over. The picture on the front is whatever the show will be about, so she chooses that too.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The One Ticket] 3. Write **ADMIT ONE** along the bottom and let her copy the shapes underneath. It stands against the kettle until she walks it round at 10:30.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Stage] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Stage] 1. Stand a chair either side of the doorway, backs facing into the room, and peg the sheet across both backs so it hangs as a curtain she can drag aside. You hang it, she tells you it is crooked.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Stage] 2. Put the cushion on the floor a few paces back. The auditorium seats one.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Stage] 3. Ask her what the show is. Take the first three answers and write them on the card in order: a song, a dance, a jump off the bottom stair.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Stage] 4. Rehearse the opening on its own. She waits behind the sheet, you say her name, she pulls the curtain and comes out. Do it four times. The coming out is the part she is learning.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Stage] 5. Run all three items once in order with the torch on her from the cushion.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Kitchen Stage] 6. Stop while she still wants more. The show is at 1:00.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Posting the Invitation] Take the ticket off the kettle and walk it to whoever is coming at 1:00. If the audience lives in this house, she posts it through your own front door and you act surprised when it lands on the mat. A grandparent up the road means the whole walk, with the letterbox flap hers to push. Gloves on. January makes that flap colder than she expects.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Posting the Invitation] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The One-Person Show] At one o'clock the ticket holder sits down, and everybody else in the house is either backstage or out of the room.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The One-Person Show] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The One-Person Show] 1. Seat the audience. One cushion, one person, biscuits and a drink in their hands. Nobody watches from the doorway.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The One-Person Show] 2. Take the delivered ticket at the door and tear the corner off. She will care about this more than any other thirty seconds of the afternoon.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The One-Person Show] 3. Announce her by her full name from behind the curtain, then get out of the way. Torch on, big light off.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The One-Person Show] 4. Watch the whole thing. No correcting the running order, no feeding her the words. Clap after each item.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The One-Person Show] 5. Ask for one of them again. An encore from an audience of one is the point of the afternoon.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The One-Person Show] 6. Swap over. She takes the cushion and the second biscuit, you go behind the curtain and do a song.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The One-Person Show] 7. Leave the stage up. She runs the show again at 4:15 with nobody watching, and that version belongs to her.
D17 (Sun Jan 17) Feeding the Birds
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Loose birdseed on the floor near a seven-month-old who mouths everything, so sweep before you put Kreston down. Wash both pairs of hands after handling seed, fat and feeders. Use smooth peanut butter only, never whole or chopped nuts, and check for a nut allergy before you start.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Window Count] **🧰 You need:** the window with the best view · paper · 1 crayon · two chairs.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Window Count] 1. Pull the chairs up to the glass and sit with her before the house is awake.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Window Count] 2. Every bird that lands in view gets a mark on the paper. She makes the mark, you keep still. Count them out loud at the end.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Window Count] 3. Ask her where a bird finds food in January with the ground frozen. Do not answer. You are making some at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Cakes] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Cakes] 1. Make a hole in the base of each pot with the skewer, thread a loop of string through and knot it inside. Yours to do, and she counts the pots.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Cakes] 2. Tip the softened fat into the bowl and let her get both hands in it. It takes her a minute to commit.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Cakes] 3. Add the seed, oats, crumbs and raisins a scoop at a time and let her squeeze until the mix holds in a lump. One part fat to two parts dry, by eye.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Cakes] 4. Pack the mix into the pots and let her press it down with her fists, string held out of the way.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Cakes] 5. Into the fridge to set. They come out at 1:00.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Cakes] 6. Wash both pairs of hands with soap before you touch the counter.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Birds Are] Walk the usual route with one job: find where the birds already are. In January they cluster in thick hedges, in anything with berries still on it, and in whatever the bramble kept. Stand still under a hedge for a minute and listen before either of you looks up. Let her choose the branch the cakes hang from on the way home.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Birds Are] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Feeding Station] The cakes come out of the fridge set hard, and the afternoon happens at the window under a blanket.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Feeding Station] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Feeding Station] 1. Run warm water round the outside of each pot and the cake slides out whole. Leave a long loop of string.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Feeding Station] 2. Hang them where she can see them from the chair: low branches, the washing line, a nail in the fence. She picks the spots, you tie the knots.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Feeding Station] 3. Stand the shallow dish in the open, clear of any bush a cat could sit under, and pour the warm water in. Unfrozen water in January is harder to find than food.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Feeding Station] 4. Scatter crumbs on the ground for the birds that will not use a feeder.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Feeding Station] 5. Come in, pull the chair to the window and get under the blanket. The wait outlasts her patience on day one.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Feeding Station] 6. Every arrival gets a mark on the tally, same as 8:00. Put the two sheets side by side at the end.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Feeding Station] 7. Leave the tally and the crayon on the windowsill. She keeps marking it all week with nobody asking.
D18 (Mon Jan 18) The Helping Day
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Hot pot on the hob with a three-year-old at counter height: pan handles turned in, she stirs from the stool with your hand on hers, and the soup goes into the jar cool enough to hold. The knives you use stay out of reach between cuts.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Job Board] **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of paper · 1 pen · crayons · masking tape.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Job Board] 1. Draw three boxes down a sheet of paper and let her fill each with a picture of the job: the potatoes, the socks, the windows.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Job Board] 2. Tape it to the kitchen door at her height, not yours.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Job Board] 3. Tell her all three are real, all three are hers, and nobody else is doing them today. She picks which goes first.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Three Real Jobs] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Three Real Jobs] 1. **The potatoes.** Bowl of warm water on the towel, stool beside it, brush in her hand. Six scrubbed until the water goes brown, and she decides when one is finished.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Three Real Jobs] 2. **The socks.** Tip the basket out and let her hunt for pairs. She will put a black one with a navy one, and those two go in the drawer as a pair.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Three Real Jobs] 3. **The windows.** Spray bottle in one hand, cloth in the other, one low window or the back door glass. Smears are the finish.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Three Real Jobs] 4. Hand each job over and move away from it. Ten minutes each is slow, and she has the whole hour.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Three Real Jobs] 5. Say what she did rather than how it turned out. "The potatoes are washed and they go in the soup at 1:00" attaches the job to something.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Three Real Jobs] 6. Do not redo any of it where she can see you. The window can wait.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Taking the Bins In] Monday, and the bins are out. Wheel yours back down the path with her hands on the handle beside yours, then knock next door and bring theirs in too. She holds on and does almost none of the pulling, and she knows it. Walk the block after and let her pick up three pieces of litter with a bag over her hand.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Taking the Bins In] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Soup for Two Houses] Today is Martin Luther King Jr. Day, marked by doing something for somebody else. Half of this pot leaves the house before dinner.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Soup for Two Houses] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Soup for Two Houses] 1. Set her up at the table with the board, the table knife and the soft vegetables. The hard ones are yours.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Soup for Two Houses] 2. Show her the grip once: fingers curled back, other hand flat on the blade, push down and forward. Then let her do it her own way.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Soup for Two Houses] 3. Everything she cuts goes in the pot, including the pieces the size of her fist. Soup forgives.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Soup for Two Houses] 4. Onto the hob with the tomatoes, the stock and the water. She stirs from the stool with your hand over hers, pan handle turned in.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Soup for Two Houses] 5. Thirty minutes of simmering, and in the gap she chooses which house gets the jar.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Soup for Two Houses] 6. Ladle half in once it has stopped steaming, screw the lid down, wrap the tea towel round it and hand it to her.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Soup for Two Houses] 7. Deliver it before dinner, or ring ahead and take it tomorrow. She hands it over herself.
D19 (Tue Jan 19) Giving It Away
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Everything that goes in Kreston's box has to be bigger than his fist. No bottle tops, no coins, no small lids. Cut the discs palm-sized, count them in and out, and the scissors stay with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Keep or Give] **🧰 You need:** 8 of her own toys · a clear bit of floor · nothing else.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Keep or Give] 1. Bring eight things she owns into the middle of the floor. You choose them, and pick ones she has not touched in a month.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Keep or Give] 2. Two piles: **keep** and **give**. She decides every one and you say nothing.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Keep or Give] 3. If all eight land in **keep**, that is the answer and you carry on. Tell her the box is going to a child who has none of this, and leave it there.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Giving Box] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Giving Box] 1. Start with clothes. Anything she has grown out of comes off the shelf, and she holds each thing once before it goes in.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Giving Box] 2. Move on to toys. She chooses. You get one veto for the whole morning, so hold onto it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Giving Box] 3. She wipes every item with the damp cloth before it goes in. She is sending these out in good condition and her hands do that part.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Giving Box] 4. Anything broken goes in the separate bag. Broken things are rubbish, and she gets to be the one who says which is which.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Giving Box] 5. Ask her for one line about each toy, meant for the child who gets it. Write it word for word on a strip of tape and stick it on: **THIS ONE ROLLS DOWN THE STAIRS.**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Giving Box] 6. Tape the box shut, let her draw on the lid, and stand it by the front door where she walks past it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Delivering the Box] The box goes today, while it still means something to her. Load it into the bottom of the pram and walk it to the charity shop, the church porch or the family two streets over with a smaller child. Let her carry it the last twenty feet even though it is too big for her, and hand it across herself. Ask whoever takes it to tell her where it goes next.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Delivering the Box] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Posting Box] Something arrives in the house this afternoon instead of leaving it, and Azlyn makes it for her brother.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Posting Box] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Posting Box] 1. Cut three or four hand-sized holes in the lid and one bigger one in the side. The scissors are yours throughout.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Posting Box] 2. Cut the cardboard into discs the size of your palm, eight to ten, counted out loud so she knows the number you started with.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Posting Box] 3. Hand her the crayons and the marker. Every disc gets decorated and so does the box. Write **KRESTON** on the lid in outline for her to fill in.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Posting Box] 4. Thread the scarf through the side hole so a length hangs out. He pulls, it keeps coming, and that is worth twenty minutes to a seven-month-old.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Posting Box] 5. Show her how to post a disc through the lid, then hand the box over and let her teach him. You stay out of it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Posting Box] 6. Sit behind them both and watch his hands. Posting is months beyond him, and the discs are still worth having.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Kreston's Posting Box] 7. Count the discs back in at the end. The same number that went out.
D20 (Wed Jan 20) Same and Different
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The hand mirror is glass, so keep it in your hand or use a plastic one, and put it away when you finish. Crayons go off the floor while Kreston is down, because a snapped one is mouth-sized. The phone stays out of his reach between calls.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Hands on the Table] **🧰 You need:** the kitchen table · her hands, yours and Kreston's · 1 hand mirror.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Hands on the Table] 1. Lay your hand flat on the table next to hers and ask what she notices. Take whatever answer arrives, including none.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Hands on the Table] 2. Bring Kreston's hand in and put all three side by side. Name the differences with nothing softened: bigger, smaller, darker, paler, more freckles.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Hands on the Table] 3. Hold the mirror so she sees her own face with yours beside it. Use the words *skin*, *hair* and *eyes* and let her use them back.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Finding the Right Crayon] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Finding the Right Crayon] 1. Tip the whole box out. Every colour on the table, nothing sorted, nothing put back.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Finding the Right Crayon] 2. She holds crayons against her own forearm one at a time until she finds the closest match. The hunting takes longer than you expect and it is the work.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Finding the Right Crayon] 3. Do the same on your arm, then on Kreston's. Three people, three crayons, and she picks all three.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Finding the Right Crayon] 4. Line the three up on the paper and let her see how close together they sit.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Finding the Right Crayon] 5. Open the photos on your phone. She names people she knows and picks a crayon for each: grandparents, the girl from playgroup, the man at the shop counter.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Finding the Right Crayon] 6. She draws everybody in the colours she chose. Write the names on for her and put one on the fridge before lunch.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Hello Walk] Walk with one job: say hello to every person you pass and keep score of how many say it back. A Wednesday morning in mid-January hands you dog walkers and the postman, so go where the people are: the parade of shops, the bus stop, the school gates at eleven. She does the hello with you a step behind her, and she counts the answers on her fingers.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Hello Walk] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Phone Calls] Three people are expecting a call from Azlyn this afternoon. She rings each one and asks the same three questions.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Phone Calls] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Phone Calls] 1. Choose the three questions together and write them big enough for her to see while she talks: **What did you have for breakfast? What is the weather like? What made you laugh today?**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Phone Calls] 2. Get on the sofa under the blanket, phone on speaker, her drawings on her lap and the crayons within reach.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Phone Calls] 3. Ring the first one. She says hello, a long silence follows, and the person at the other end has been warned to wait through it. She asks the questions in her own order and forgets the third: prompt once, then leave it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Phone Calls] 4. Write each answer down as it comes and read the three back to her once you have hung up.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Phone Calls] 5. She holds her drawing of that person up to the phone, which makes no sense and matters to her. Tell them what she drew, then she starts a fresh drawing of whoever is next while the number rings.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Phone Calls] 6. Lay the three sheets side by side once the calls are done and find the one thing all three people said. There is always one.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Three Phone Calls] 7. Turn each sheet over and let her draw what she was told: the weather at their window, the breakfast, the thing that made them laugh.
D21 (Thu Jan 21) Thank You Day
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The oven is on and the tray comes out at her eye level. She stays back from the door, the hot tray goes to the middle of the counter, and Kreston stays out of the kitchen while it moves. Raw dough is not a snack today.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Reading the Jar] **🧰 You need:** the Good Thing Jar · the strips · the kitchen table.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Reading the Jar] 1. Tip the whole jar out and let her push the strips around with both hands. There should be twenty or more.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Reading the Jar] 2. Read every one out loud. All of them, including the four that say the same thing.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Reading the Jar] 3. Sort them into piles, one per person, and count each pile out loud. That count is how many biscuits get cut at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Thank-You Biscuits] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Thank-You Biscuits] 1. Butter and sugar in the bowl, flour on top. She rubs it through with her fingertips until the bowl looks like breadcrumbs. Both hands in, no spoon.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Thank-You Biscuits] 2. Add the milk a spoon at a time and let her squeeze it into a dough. A dusting of flour fixes it if it sticks to her.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Thank-You Biscuits] 3. Flour the board and roll it out to about a finger's thickness. Leave the thin patches where they are.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Thank-You Biscuits] 4. She counts the shapes against the piles from 8:00 and keeps cutting until the numbers match. Re-roll the scraps twice, then the third lot is hers.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Thank-You Biscuits] 5. Bake at 180°C for 12 to 15 minutes, until the edges turn gold. She stays at the table. You move the tray.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Thank-You Biscuits] 6. Slide them onto the cold plate once they firm up, and count them again with her when they are cool.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Biscuit Round] Bag the biscuits in twos and walk them round. The neighbour who took the parcel in, the woman at the corner shop, the friend who has had a rough month. She knocks, she hands them over, she says her own version of the words. Ten minutes a stop, so pick three doors and stop at three. Keep two back for the walk home.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Biscuit Round] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Thank-You Cards] Every strip in that jar has a name on it, and every one of those people gets a card this afternoon.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Thank-You Cards] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Thank-You Cards] 1. Cut the card into rectangles and fold them. Cereal boxes work fine and the printed side goes on the inside.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Thank-You Cards] 2. Take the strips one person at a time. Read them out loud, then ask her what the card should say.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Thank-You Cards] 3. Write her words down word for word. **THANK YOU FOR CARRYING ME UP THE STAIRS WHEN MY LEGS WERE TIRED.** Do not shorten it or fix the grammar.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Thank-You Cards] 4. She draws the thing they did on the front. The drawing will be unrecognisable and the person will recognise it, because her words sit underneath.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Thank-You Cards] 5. She signs each one: a shape, a letter A, a single line, the same mark on every card so it becomes her signature.
