    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Thank-You Cards] 6. Stickers and glue on whatever needs them, then stack the finished cards next to the biscuit bags by the door.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Thank-You Cards] 7. One card goes in the post with a stamp on it. Walk to the postbox before dinner and let her drop it in.
D22 (Fri Jan 22) The Seven Pieces
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Seven small card pieces and a 7-month-old who mouths everything do not go together. Build on the table, sweep before you put Kreston down, and count the pieces back into the envelope every time. The scissors are adult only and go back up out of reach between cuts, not onto the table where she is working.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Seven Pieces] **🧰 You need:** 1 cereal-box card cut to a 20 cm square · a ruler or any straight edge · 1 pencil · scissors (adult only).
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Seven Pieces] 1. Rule a line corner to corner and cut along it. Two big triangles. She holds the card flat while you cut.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Seven Pieces] 2. Cut one of those in half again, then work the other down into a medium triangle, two small ones, a square and a slanted four-sided piece.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Seven Pieces] 3. Seven pieces on the table and no picture yet. Let her push them round, then leave them until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Tangram Animals] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Tangram Animals] 1. Hand her all seven and ask her to make the square they came from. Leave her with it a while. She does not have to get it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Tangram Animals] 2. Build a cat together on a sheet of paper: big triangle for the body, the square for the head, two small triangles for ears. She sets the ears.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Tangram Animals] 3. Draw round the finished cat in pencil, lift the pieces off and go over the outline in marker.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Tangram Animals] 4. Do a boat and a house the same way, tracing each one before you break it up.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Tangram Animals] 5. Let her invent the fourth. Trace it and write her name for it along the bottom, spelling and all.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Tangram Animals] 6. Everything goes in the envelope with **TANGRAM** on the front. She is back at it by 4:15.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Arrow Trail] Walk out with a job: at every corner she lays an arrow of sticks, stones or
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Arrow Trail] scuffed gravel pointing the way you came. Four or five is plenty. On the way
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Arrow Trail] back she reads her own arrows and gets you home. The ground is frozen hard, so
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Arrow Trail] they sit where she put them. Check tomorrow.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Arrow Trail] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] A second set of seven comes off the other cereal box: two builders, two trays,
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] a timer.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] 1. Cut the second set with her calling out which piece comes next. She watched at 8:00 and remembers more of the order than you expect.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] 2. One set on each tray, sitting opposite each other with the timer between you.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] 3. Two minutes on the clock. Build a picture on your tray without showing her while she builds one on hers.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] 4. Slide your tray alongside hers with your picture still standing on it. She clears her own tray and copies yours piece by piece. Set the timer again.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] 5. She will get stuck. Let her pick her tray up and turn it round rather than point at the piece she needs.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] 6. One round from memory, three pieces only. Build a small shape, give her a slow count of five, drop the tea towel over it and let her go. Three she can hold in her head at this age. Asking for seven ends the afternoon.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Puzzle Swap] 7. Let her set the timer for the last round, then tip both sets into one pile and build one enormous animal.
D23 (Sat Jan 23) The Puzzle That Says Something
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Ten jigsaw pieces and a 7-month-old on the floor means the tray stays at table height and the count goes back in the bag every time. Glue sticks come apart in a mouth, so the lid goes on and the stick goes up whenever you leave the table. Crayons snap into mouth-sized pieces, so keep your hand round Kreston's for his ten seconds and put the crayon straight back in the tin. The scissors are adult only.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing It Onto the Card] **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of cereal-box card · crayons · 1 printed photo of her, in case the drawing does not happen · 1 glue stick.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing It Onto the Card] 1. Give her the card blank side up and ask for a drawing that goes to the edges. Big shapes, heavy colour, no white left. A photo does the same job if she is not in the mood.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing It Onto the Card] 2. Sit and draw your own beside her without commenting on hers.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing It Onto the Card] 3. Tell her what happens at 9:15: you cut it into pieces. Let her add anything she wants first.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Jigsaw with Words on the Back] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**

D24 (Sun Jan 24) Getting It Open
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Elastic bands, raisins and a ziplock bag are hazards for a 7-month-old. Work at the table, count the six treasures in and out, and pick the band up the moment it comes off.     [🎨 The Main Event: Six Ways to Open a Thing] 2. Count to twenty before you offer anything, then offer a word and not a hand: *turning*, or *find the corner*.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Six Ways to Open a Thing] 3. The screw lid is hardest, because two hands go opposite ways at once. If she is stuck, she holds the lid still and turns the jar.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Six Ways to Open a Thing] 4. The knotted string will beat her. Loosen it to one loop and hand it back so the last pull is hers.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Six Ways to Open a Thing] 5. When all six are open, count the treasures. Shut them again, and she picks which fastening goes on which.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Six Ways to Open a Thing] 6. Now she loads them and hands you the tray. Struggle out loud with the elastic band.

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Gate Latch Walk] gate with the drop bolt, the churchyard latch, your back gate, the catch on
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Gate Latch Walk] the recycling bin, the brake on the pram. Cold metal takes a firmer hand than
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Gate Latch Walk] a plastic lid.

D25 (Mon Jan 25) The Memory Tray
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Count the sixteen cards into the shoebox lid and out of it every time, and keep the tray of objects up at table height while Kreston is on the floor.     [🌅 Opening Activity: Filling the Tray] 2. Lay them out in two rows of four and name each one out loud. Let her shuffle them until it looks right to her.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Filling the Tray] 3. Cover the tray with the tea towel and leave it on the table. Say nothing about what happens next.
    [🎨 The Main Event: What's Missing from the Tray] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: What's Missing from the Tray] 1. Uncover the tray and name all eight, pointing at each. Give her a slow count of ten.
    [🎨 The Main Event: What's Missing from the Tray] 2. Cover it. She turns round or shuts her eyes. Take one object off and sit on it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: What's Missing from the Tray] 3. Uncover and let her name what has gone. If she stalls, name the ones still there and let her hear the gap.
    [🎨 The Main Event: What's Missing from the Tray] 4. Drop to six objects if eight is too many, go up to ten when eight stops being work.
    [🎨 The Main Event: What's Missing from the Tray] 5. Swap over. She takes one while you look away, and you work it out loud: *sock, apple, book, and the spoon was next to the book*.
    [🎨 The Main Event: What's Missing from the Tray] 6. Keep score in tally marks, hers and yours, and leave the tray out so she can run it on whoever walks in.
    [🌳 Get Outside: She Leads the Way Home] Walk ten minutes out from the door, taking three or four turns, then hand the
    [🌳 Get Outside: She Leads the Way Home] job over: she gets you home. Name what you pass on the way out, the red door,
    [🌳 Get Outside: She Leads the Way Home] the postbox, the dog behind the fence. January sun sits low enough to change
    [🌳 Get Outside: She Leads the Way Home] how the street looks coming back, so expect one wrong turn and let her walk
    [🌳 Get Outside: She Leads the Way Home] into it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: She Leads the Way Home] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pairs She Draws Herself] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Pairs She Draws Herself] 1. Lay the sixteen cards out blank side up and tell her the job: eight things, each drawn twice, so every card has a twin.

    [🎨 The Main Event: Sorting It Twice] 4. Same objects, new rule: things that need two hands, things that fit in one. Two bowls. She will complain about starting again, then beat her own time.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Sorting It Twice] 5. Third round is hers. She sorts by a rule she keeps secret and you work it out from what lands in each bowl. Guess wrong twice before you get there.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Sorting It Twice] 6. Her rule will be *things I like* and *things I do not*. Both count. Write it on the paper, read it back and tape it to the bowl.

    [🌳 Get Outside: Two Bags, One Rule] brown leaves, bare twigs, a seed head, a bottle top, gravel. The arguments are
    [🌳 Get Outside: Two Bags, One Rule] the good part. Ten minutes of collecting is plenty in this cold.

    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tree That Fills the Wall] 1. Tape the torn strips to the wall as a trunk, floor to shoulder height, wider at the bottom. Overlap them until no wall shows.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tree That Fills the Wall] 2. Add branches from the shorter strips. She decides how many and where, and crooked ones stay crooked.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tree That Fills the Wall] 3. Tear leaves from the green paper or magazine pages and tape them along the branches in handfuls.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tree That Fills the Wall] 4. Draw a line along the skirting board and run roots below it. That line is the ground.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tree That Fills the Wall] 5. Sort the animals. Ask where each one lives: roots, trunk, branches, top. Tape a card shelf at that height and stand it there.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Tree That Fills the Wall] 6. Write the four layers up the wall in marker: **roots, trunk, branches, top.** Read them out with her, bottom up.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Choosing Her Tree] Walk the route and let her pick one tree to be hers for the year. She has to
    [🌳 Get Outside: Choosing Her Tree] give a reason, and any reason counts. Put both arms round the trunk to see
    [🌳 Get Outside: Choosing Her Tree] whether they meet, then count the big branches. Photograph her against it
    [🌳 Get Outside: Choosing Her Tree] today, the last Saturday in January, and again in April, July and October.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Choosing Her Tree] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Forest Tour] She knows where every animal on that wall lives. This afternoon she says it

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Forest Tour] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Forest Tour] 1. Set the chairs in a row facing the tree with a teddy in every one. Kreston sits at the end, propped against the cushion.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Forest Tour] 2. Make tickets. She scribbles on the paper scraps and hands one to every seat, Kreston included.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Forest Tour] 3. Give her the tube and the torch. The tube is her voice and the torch is her pointer.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Small stones, corks and bottle tops sit at mouth height for a 7-month-old. D32 (Mon Feb 1) Who Came Past in the Night
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Flour on a hard floor is slippery in a way that puts adults down as fast as children. Sweep between rounds, keep it off tile and laminate near the stairs, and never lay a trail on a staircase. Sieve it rather than throwing it, so nobody breathes a cloud.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Four Feet] **🧰 You need:** paper · 1 crayon · the 2 paw stamps · one shut door.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Four Feet] 1. Draw four feet on one sheet: a bird's, a duck's, a grown-up's, and a paw with four toes. Draw them rough; she will still read them.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Four Feet] 2. Ask whose is whose and take the wrong answers without fixing them. Then ask what each foot is good at. Gripping, swimming, walking, digging.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Four Feet] 3. Press one paw stamp onto the paper dry so she meets the shape before the trail. Something came through the house in the night, and that door stays shut until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Track Trail] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Track Trail] 1. Open the door and stand back. Let her look before anybody says a word.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Track Trail] 2. Follow it from the start on hands and knees. Count the prints out loud and let her take over.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Track Trail] 3. Stop at every clue and ask the same question: what does this tell us. A chewed leaf says it eats plants, a tuft of wool says it is furry, crumbs say it went past the bin.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Track Trail] 4. Find the animal at the end. She lifts whatever it is hiding under and says the name.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Track Trail] 5. Now walk it backwards, animal to door, and ask which way it was going. This is the hard part.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Track Trail] 6. Sweep up together. Keep half a cup of flour back for after lunch.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Footprints in the Frost] Early February holds frost on grass and car roofs until the sun comes round.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Footprints in the Frost] Walk the shaded side of the street and read what crossed it in the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Footprints in the Frost] night: a cat's prints along a wall, bird feet in a frozen puddle, her own boots
    [🌳 Get Outside: Footprints in the Frost] on white grass. Send her across a frosted lawn, then turn her round to see
    [🌳 Get Outside: Footprints in the Frost] what she left.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Footprints in the Frost] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph] Joseph walks in at six tonight with no idea there is anything on the floor.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph] Azlyn spends the afternoon making sure there is.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph] 1. Walk the route with her first, no flour, and let her fix where it starts and ends. Follow her decision even when it goes through the kitchen twice.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph] 2. She chooses what waits at the end and hides it herself, out of your sight.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph] 3. Print the prints. She presses the stamp, you sieve a little flour onto the sponge between presses.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph] 4. Make three arrow cards. She draws them, you tape them at Joseph's eye height rather than hers.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Laying a Trail for Joseph] 5. Add two clues of her own. Ask what each is meant to tell him and write her answer on the back.

D33 (Tue Feb 2) Feeding the Birds
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Melt the fat to soft and never to liquid, and let it cool until you can hold a finger in it before her hands go near. Whole nuts are a choking hazard, and peanut butter is out if there is any nut allergy in the house. The string gets tied outside and the ball comes straight back indoors, because a loop of string at toddler height is a strangulation risk. The binoculars stay stringless for the same reason: no cord long enough to go over her head, on those or on anything else in the house. The skewer stays with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bird Menu] **🧰 You need:** paper · crayons · a saucer of seed, oats and raisins.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bird Menu] 1. Put the saucer in front of her and let her sort it. Seeds here, oats there, raisins in their own heap.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bird Menu] 2. Ask which a bird would go for first. Write her answer as a menu, one line per item, with her drawing of the bird beside it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bird Menu] 3. Pin the menu on the window the feeders hang outside. She checks it against real birds all week.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Café] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Café] 1. Make the holes first, while your hands are clean. Push the skewer through the base of each pot, thread string through and knot it inside.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Café] 2. Warm the fat to soft, well short of clear, and let it stand until you can hold a finger in it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Café] 3. Tip the seed into the bowl, add the fat, and let her work them together with a spoon and then both hands. Let this part run long.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Café] 4. Pack the mix into the pots and press it down hard with her thumbs, up to the rim.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Café] 5. Roll a pine cone in what is left and tie a string round the top.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bird Café] 6. Stand them in the fridge while you have snack. They go outside at 10:30, and any still soft go out when you get back from swimming.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Hanging the Feeders] Hang all four where she can see them from a window and a cat cannot reach. A
    [🌳 Get Outside: Hanging the Feeders] washing line, a bare branch, a hook under the eaves, a fence post. She picks
    [🌳 Get Outside: Hanging the Feeders] every spot and you lift her to the high ones. Count from inside how many she
    [🌳 Get Outside: Hanging the Feeders] can see. February is the hard month out there, and this one gets found inside
    [🌳 Get Outside: Hanging the Feeders] a week.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Hanging the Feeders] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart] Nothing will come to those feeders today. Birds take a day or two to find a
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart] new one, so build her somewhere to sit and wait.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart] 1. Tape two tubes side by side for her pair, and two more for yours. Nothing goes round her neck: she holds hers to her eyes with both hands.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart] 2. Let her colour both pairs.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart] 3. Build the window seat: cushions under the window, blanket over her knees, chart and pencil beside her.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart] 4. Draw the chart with her. Five rows, one bird per row, a tick box at the end.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart] 5. Sit and watch. Ten minutes is a long time at three, so set the deal now: watch, something else, watch again.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Binoculars and the Bird Chart] 6. Tick nothing, and treat that as an answer. Ask why and write her reason at the bottom.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Kreston is 7 months and cannot get himself out of anywhere. He goes in the cave only with you in it, and never on his own for a second.     [🎨 The Main Event: The Bear Cave] 2. Tear the paper into leaves together. Fill a carrier bag and let the tearing run as long as she wants.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears] spoon and a pan lid, and winter ends whenever she says it does.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears] 1. Everybody hibernates. Into the cave or under a blanket, eyes shut, and hold it while you count to twenty out loud.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears] 2. Bang the pan lid once with the spoon. That is spring. Everybody wakes, stretches and comes out slow.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears] 3. Go round again with her holding the spoon. She decides how long winter lasts, three seconds the first time and two minutes the fourth.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears] 4. Change one rule each round. Sleep standing up. Sleep in a pile. Sleep with the torch on. Sleep and snore.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears] 5. Creep past a sleeping bear without waking it, then swap and let her creep past you. Getting caught is the fun of it, so get caught.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears] 6. Take the milk and the snack into the cave and eat in there with the torch on.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Waking the Bears] 7. Read the books in the cave and finish with everybody asleep and nobody banging anything.
D35 (Thu Feb 4) The Shadow Forest
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Loose lolly sticks and straws stay off the floor while Kreston is down. They splinter, and they fit down his throat, so count them onto the table and back off it. Card this thick is yours to cut and the scissors stay with you. Peg the sheet where it can come down without bringing anything with it, a door frame rather than a curtain pole or a shelf. Stand the torch on the floor instead of a stack of books, and with the room lights off keep Kreston inside arm's reach the whole time.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Guess the Shadow] **🧰 You need:** 1 torch · a pale wall · 3 toy animals · your hands.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: Guess the Shadow] 2. Put one animal in the beam. Ask which one it is before she turns round.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Guess the Shadow] 3. Move it closer to the torch and let the shadow swell. Hand her the torch until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shadow Animals] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shadow Animals] 1. Draw the four animals on the cereal box: fox, owl, deer, hedgehog. Big simple outlines, no detail inside. You draw, she directs.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shadow Animals] 2. Cut them out. Card this thick is adult work: she holds the box flat while you cut.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shadow Animals] 3. Tape a lolly stick low on the back of each, so her hand stays out of the beam.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shadow Animals] 4. Test the first one against the wall. If the ears are too small or the legs have joined into a block, cut wider and test again.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shadow Animals] 5. Do the same to all four. Deer antlers have to be fat, and an owl needs a notch between its ear tufts.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Shadow Animals] 6. Line them up and test from across the room. Any animal she cannot name goes back for cutting.

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Longest Shadow] tit comes back to finish it if you hold still. Let her tick

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Longest Shadow] shadows run long. Stand her on the pavement at eleven, draw round her shadow
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Longest Shadow] in chalk, and pace it out in her steps. Come back past the chalk mark on the
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Longest Shadow] way home.

D36 (Fri Feb 5) The Dough That Wakes Up
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Kreston is crawling and the oven runs at 220°C today, with hot trays coming out at his head height. Block his route to the oven door with a chair or the pram before the dough goes in, and check it is still there before you open the door. Never leave the oven door open to cool with him loose in the room.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Fizzing Jar] **🧰 You need:** 1 clean jam jar · dried yeast, 1 sachet · sugar, 1 tsp · warm water, 100 ml · 1 teaspoon.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Fizzing Jar] 1. Fill the jar a third full from the hot tap with cold run in until it feels like bath water on your wrist. Let her test it with one finger.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Fizzing Jar] 2. Tip in the yeast and the sugar and let her stir. Then walk away for ten minutes.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Fizzing Jar] 3. Come back and look. The top has gone beige and foamy and smells of beer. Tell her the yeast is alive and has been eating the sugar.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Things Make Bread] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Things Make Bread] 1. Count the four things on her fingers before anything goes in the bowl: flour, water, salt, yeast.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Things Make Bread] 2. Flour into the bowl, salt to one side, and let her punch a well in the middle. The yeast goes in the well, then the oil and the warm water.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Things Make Bread] 3. She mixes with one hand until it comes together as a shaggy lump. It sticks to her. Leave it sticking.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Things Make Bread] 4. Turn it onto the table and knead: push with the heel of your hand, fold, turn a quarter, push again. Say the four words out loud and hand it over.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Things Make Bread] 5. Ten minutes of that between you. She will manage two and wander off, so take it on and hand it back when she returns. The lump goes from ragged to smooth.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Things Make Bread] 6. Oil the bowl, drop the dough in, cover it and stand it somewhere warm. Mark its height on the outside with a strip of tape.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Walk Past the Bakery] Walk to whichever shop near you bakes on the premises. The supermarket counter
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Walk Past the Bakery] counts. Cold February air holds the smell tight to the doorway, so the last
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Walk Past the Bakery] four steps are the good ones. Count the shapes through the glass: rounds, long
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Walk Past the Bakery] ones, plaits, rolls with seeds. Buy one thing and let her carry it home in her
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Walk Past the Bakery] own hand.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Walk Past the Bakery] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock] The dough has doubled by one o'clock and it holds the dent when she pushes a
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock] finger in.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock] 1. Tip the dough onto a floured table and let her press all the air out of it. It sighs down and she will want to do it twice.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock] 2. Cut it into eight or ten pieces. She cuts, you even them up afterwards without saying so.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock] 3. Show her the roll: hand cupped over the piece, palm circling, dough turning underneath. Hers come out as lumps and lumps bake fine.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock] 4. Space them on the lined tray a hand's width apart, cover, and leave them thirty minutes. Oven on to 220°C.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock] 5. Brush the tops with milk and bake fifteen to eighteen minutes. She watches through the glass and the door stays shut.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Rolls and the Hollow Knock] 6. Out onto the rack. Once one has cooled enough to hold, turn it over and knock the bottom with a knuckle. Hollow means baked through, so let her knock every one.
D37 (Sat Feb 6) Azlyn's Own Recipe
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Raw egg and raw flour both carry a real infection risk and she will eat some of the mixture. Hold it to one small spoonful, or set a spoonful aside before the egg goes in. Kreston gets none of it raw, and biscuits are sugar all the way through, so he skips the baked ones too. The bowl goes in the sink and not on the floor.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing Off the Shelf] **🧰 You need:** the baking cupboard · a stool to stand on · paper and a pen.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing Off the Shelf] 1. Open the cupboard and get her up to eye level with the shelf. Read out what is in each tub and let her hold the ones with lids.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing Off the Shelf] 2. She picks the additions for her recipe. If that is sprinkles and mashed banana together, it is sprinkles and mashed banana together.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Choosing Off the Shelf] 3. Write her choices at the top of a fresh page under her name. Nothing gets vetoed.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Recipe She Tells You] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Recipe She Tells You] 1. Sit down with her and ask how it is made, starting with the first thing into the bowl.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Recipe She Tells You] 2. Write every word as it comes. *And then you shake it a lot.* *Two hundred sugars.* Do not tidy the order or fix the grammar.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Recipe She Tells You] 3. Read it back from the top. She will change things. Cross out and write the new version underneath so both stay.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Recipe She Tells You] 4. Now make it off her page. Quantities come from the list above, order comes from her.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Recipe She Tells You] 5. Roll the mixture into walnut-sized balls, flatten them onto the lined tray with gaps between, and she adds her extras where her page says.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Recipe She Tells You] 6. Bake at 180°C for twelve to fifteen minutes until the edges brown. Read her recipe out loud once more while they bake.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Frost Patch Hunt] Go out before the sun reaches the shady side of the street. Frost survives
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Frost Patch Hunt] where it has not: north-facing walls, the ground under parked cars, grass in
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Frost Patch Hunt] the shadow of a hedge. Give her five patches to find and a fingernail to
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Frost Patch Hunt] scratch each one. Put her bare palm flat on a frosted car roof for one second,
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Frost Patch Hunt] then back in the mitten.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Frost Patch Hunt] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook] This afternoon her recipe becomes a page in a book with her name on the front.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook] 1. Trim the sheets to a size her hands can turn and lay them in order.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook] 2. Tape the morning's recipe onto the middle page, butter stain and crossings-out and all.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook] 3. She draws one ingredient per sheet on the pages before it: butter, sugar, egg, flour, her own addition. Write the word underneath in your own hand, big.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook] 4. Cut the cereal box flat for the cover and let her colour it. Ask her the name of the book and write it across the front word for word.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook] 5. Punch two holes down the left edge of every sheet and thread the string. Three staples down the spine work as well.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook] 6. Read it back cover to cover, turning the pages at her pace.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Her Page in the Cookbook] 7. Stand it on the kitchen shelf with the real cookbooks, at her height.
D38 (Sun Feb 7) Pizza by Hand
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The oven goes to 240°C today and Kreston is shuffling, so block his route to it with a chair or the pram before the first tray goes in, and check the block is still there before you open the door. Melted cheese holds its heat long after the crust has cooled. Cut hers into strips and leave them three minutes on the board before the plate reaches her. There will be ice underfoot on the walk, so take the gritted side of the path and keep one hand free for her.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Punching It Down] **🧰 You need:** the overnight dough, cold from the fridge · flour · a tea towel.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Punching It Down] 1. Take the bowl out and let her look first. The dough has climbed the sides overnight and smells sour.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Punching It Down] 2. Give her one instruction: fist in, all the way. The whole thing sighs to a third of its height and she will laugh.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Punching It Down] 3. Leave it under the tea towel to come to room temperature. Cold dough will not stretch, so the wait is an hour.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Dough That Fights Back] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Dough That Fights Back] 1. Flour the table well. Cut the dough in half and give her one half.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Dough That Fights Back] 2. Show her both flat hands pushing out from the middle, then work yours.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Dough That Fights Back] 3. She will tear hers. Pinch it shut, carry on, and say out loud that dough mends and paper does not.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Dough That Fights Back] 4. Once it is about tray-sized, lift an edge and let it hang from her hands. It stretches under its own weight and springs back when she lays it down. Let her try yours as well.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Dough That Fights Back] 5. Lay both bases on the oiled trays and push them to the corners with her fingertips. Bumpy is right.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Dough That Fights Back] 6. Cover them with the tea towel until one o'clock.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping the Ice] Take the route with puddles. February ice is thin and goes white where air got
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping the Ice] trapped underneath. It cracks under a welly with a sound she will chase down
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping the Ice] the path. Stamp one, then lift a plate of ice out of another and hold it
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping the Ice] against the sky. Look for the frozen rim round a drain. Wet gloves come home in
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping the Ice] a pocket.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stamping the Ice] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Pizza Each] Everything comes to the table in small bowls at one o'clock and nobody builds
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Pizza Each] anybody else's.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Pizza Each] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Pizza Each] 1. Set the bowls in a line down the table, a spoon standing in each.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Pizza Each] 2. Sauce first. Show her the spoon spiralling out from the middle, then let her do her own. The edges she misses stay missed.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Pizza Each] 3. Cheese by the handful. Everyone works down the line taking what they want, and nobody comments.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Pizza Each] 4. She will build yours as well. Eat whatever she puts on it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Pizza Each] 5. Oven as hot as it goes, 240°C if you can get it. One tray at a time, top shelf, ten to twelve minutes, until the edges blister.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Pizza Each] 6. Out onto the board, herbs over the top, and cut hers into strips.
D39 (Mon Feb 8) The Bakery Opens
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Playdough carries far more salt than food and Kreston will have it in his mouth the second he is near the tray. Keep the tray above his reach and wipe the table before he goes on the floor. Buttons and bottle tops are choking-sized for an 8-month-old. Count them into the purse at the start and back into the till at closing.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Colouring the Dough] **🧰 You need:** plain flour, 2 cups · salt, 1 cup · warm water, 1 cup · oil, 2 tbsp · food colouring · 1 big bowl.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Colouring the Dough] 1. Flour and salt in the bowl, her hands in to mix them dry. Then oil and warm water, stirred until it leaves the sides.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Colouring the Dough] 2. Knead it on the table for two minutes while it is warm, then split it into three.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Colouring the Dough] 3. Press a hollow into each piece, three drops of a different colour into each, and let her fold it in. Her hands come out patchy and wash clean by lunch.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Twelve Pretend Pastries] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Twelve Pretend Pastries] 1. Line the tray with the cake cases first, so it looks like a shop window.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Twelve Pretend Pastries] 2. Croissants: roll a ball, flatten it, cut a triangle, roll from the wide end and curve the ends in. Do one, hand her the knife, let hers come out fat.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Twelve Pretend Pastries] 3. Plaits: three snakes rolled under her flat palms, pinched at the top and crossed over one another.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Twelve Pretend Pastries] 4. Buns and rings: balls pressed with the back of the fork, and snakes joined end to end for doughnuts.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Twelve Pretend Pastries] 5. Keep going until the tray is full. Count them on out loud, one to twelve, then backwards when the dough runs out.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Twelve Pretend Pastries] 6. Stand the tray on the counter you are about to build. None of it gets baked and she will ask twice.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Mud Bakery] Take an old baking tray, a wooden spoon and a plastic tub to the muddiest
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Mud Bakery] patch you can reach. February mud is soft to the top of a welly and cold enough
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Mud Bakery] to hold a shape. She makes mud pastries to match the ones on the kitchen
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Mud Bakery] counter and counts them the same way. Leave them there for the frost.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Mud Bakery] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Counter Opens] A dining chair turned side-on is the counter. She takes the inside, you take
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Counter Opens] the pavement side and the customer's part for the afternoon.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Counter Opens] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Counter Opens] 1. Set the counter where she says. Tray on top, shoebox till beside it, tea towel in her waistband as the apron.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Counter Opens] 2. Write the price signs with her, a number and a drawing on each, and she decides every number.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Counter Opens] 3. Be the first customer. Say good morning, ask for two things by name and wait for her to name a price.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Counter Opens] 4. Pay in buttons, one at a time, counted out loud into her hand. She puts them in the till and shuts the lid.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Counter Opens] 5. Bag the order and let it take as long as it takes. Folding the top over twice is the part she will want again.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Counter Opens] 6. Swap over. She comes in with the purse and you get one item of her order wrong on purpose.

D40 (Tue Feb 9) What Yeast Eats
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Balloons are the worst choking hazard in this house for an 8-month-old, whole or burst, and a burst piece moulds to the airway where nothing can shift it. Keep the spares in your pocket, count them out and count them back in, and bin any that split. Run the cold in first and test the water on your wrist before her finger goes near it, because a hot tap on its own will scald. At the pool stay within arm's reach of both of them, and that means arm's reach and not eyeline.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bath-Water Test] **🧰 You need:** 2 cups or mugs · the cold tap and the hot · 1 jug.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bath-Water Test] 1. Fill one cup from the cold tap and the second from the hot, cold run in until it feels like bath water on your wrist.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bath-Water Test] 2. She puts a finger in each and gives both a word. Warm as a bath is what yeast wants and anything hotter kills it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bath-Water Test] 3. Pour the warm one into the jug and stand it on the side. It goes into the bottles at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Balloon That Fills Itself] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Balloon That Fills Itself] 1. Stand the bottles side by side and let her mark them 1 and 2.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Balloon That Fills Itself] 2. Bottle 1 gets a sachet of yeast and a teaspoon of sugar down the funnel, then warm water. Bottle 2 gets warm water only. Draw a line on both at the same height.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Balloon That Fills Itself] 3. She swirls bottle 1 until the yeast clouds the water. Bottle 2 gets the same swirling and stays clear.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Balloon That Fills Itself] 4. Stretch a balloon over each neck, right down over the shoulder so it grips.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Balloon That Fills Itself] 5. Stand them somewhere warm and look at the clock together, then go outside.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Balloon That Fills Itself] 6. Back at half eleven, bottle 1's balloon stands up and bottle 2's is flat. Let her squeeze both and ask which had food in it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Snowdrop Hunt] Snowdrops are up by the second week of February and come back in the same
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Snowdrop Hunt] places every year: churchyards, the foot of a hedge, the bare ground under a
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Snowdrop Hunt] tree. Give her a number and walk until she has counted twenty. Get down to
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Snowdrop Hunt] their height, because the flower hangs face down and the markings are
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Snowdrop Hunt] underneath. Nothing gets picked.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Snowdrop Hunt] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Shaking Cream into Butter] A jar, a tight lid, and everybody in the house taking a turn.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Shaking Cream into Butter] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Shaking Cream into Butter] 1. Pour the cream in until the jar is half full, no further, so there is air above it. Screw the lid down hard and check it twice.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Shaking Cream into Butter] 2. Everybody shakes. Count to fifty each and pass it on. Azlyn's fifty will be short and loud and she gets all the turns she wants.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Shaking Cream into Butter] 3. Around five minutes in it goes thick and the sloshing stops. That is whipped cream, and she is halfway.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Shaking Cream into Butter] 4. Keep going through the quiet stretch. The sound comes back as a heavier thud, then a lump rolling round in liquid. Open the jar and let her look.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Shaking Cream into Butter] 5. Strain it through the sieve over the bowl. The lump is butter, the liquid is buttermilk and she can drink some.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Shaking Cream into Butter] 6. Squeeze the butter under cold water with the back of a spoon until it runs clear, work in the salt and press it onto the cold plate.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Shaking Cream into Butter] 7. Spread it on bread and eat it at the counter. What is left goes in the fridge for tomorrow.
D41 (Wed Feb 10) Five Kinds of Bread
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** No honey for Kreston before he is one. Infant botulism is rare and serious, and honey turns up in seeded loaves, malted bread and crumpet toppings, so read the bag. Seeds, crusts and torn bagel are all choking-sized for him at 8 months, so his plate is soft white with the crust off and nothing else.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Numbering the Plates] **🧰 You need:** 5 plates · 1 marker or 5 sticky labels · the five breads · a bread knife (**adult**).
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Numbering the Plates] 1. Lay the five plates in a row and let her put a number on each. Write yours underneath hers if the numbers come out as shapes.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Numbering the Plates] 2. You cut and she plates. One piece of each bread onto its own numbered plate, each piece the size of a bite.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Numbering the Plates] 3. Cover the whole row with a tea towel and walk away. She will lift the corner twice before nine.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Bread Wins] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Bread Wins] 1. Sit her down with the row in front of her and the cup of water beside it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Bread Wins] 2. Plate 1 first. One bite, chew, a mouthful of water, then talk about it before plate 2 comes near her.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Bread Wins] 3. Ask what her tongue found. Soft, crunchy, holey, chewy, seedy. Write her word next to the number, and hers will include *spiky* and *like a pillow*.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Bread Wins] 4. Work along to plate 5 with water between each one. No doubling back until the row is finished.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Bread Wins] 5. Hand her the stickers or a crayon. Every bread she would eat again gets a mark, as many as she likes.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Bread Wins] 6. Count the marks together and say the winner out loud, then eat the rest of it with yesterday's butter.
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Slow Lap of the Block] Walk the block at her pace, which turns a five-minute loop into twenty, and
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Slow Lap of the Block] let her set every stop. This is the week the buds show on the hedges, hard
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Slow Lap of the Block] little points on bare stems, so put her fingers on one and then on a bare
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Slow Lap of the Block] stem. Count the front doors down one side and look over every wall for the
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Slow Lap of the Block] first green thing in anybody's garden.
    [🌳 Get Outside: One Slow Lap of the Block] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bakery Window] The front window faces the street. Fill it from the inside and the house reads
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bakery Window] as a bakery to anyone walking past.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bakery Window] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bakery Window] 1. Lay the paper out and start with loaves. Show her the long shape and the round shape once each, then leave her to it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bakery Window] 2. Buns, plaits, cakes with something on top. Big drawings work better in a window than accurate ones.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bakery Window] 3. Cut each one out along her line while she draws the next. She fills sheets faster than you can cut.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bakery Window] 4. Ask her the name of the shop. Write it across a sheet in fat marker, saying each letter as you make it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bakery Window] 5. Tape the sign up first, top of the window, facing out to the street.

D42 (Thu Feb 11) Delivery Day
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The floss and the string go back in the drawer the moment the cutting and the tying are done. A loose length of either is a strangulation risk at 8 months and Kreston is on the floor all afternoon. The tin comes out of a 190°C oven, so stand it at the back of the counter where he cannot pull it down, and give it an hour before anyone lifts it bare-handed. Put Azlyn on the stool for the rolling rather than a dining chair, since she leans her whole weight onto that pin.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Cinnamon Butter] **🧰 You need:** soft butter, 75 g · sugar, 75 g · ground cinnamon, 2 tsp · 1 small bowl · 1 fork · her cookbook from Saturday.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Cinnamon Butter] 1. Stand her cookbook open at the butter page and let her find the word. Butter, sugar and cinnamon into the bowl, and she mashes it to a brown paste.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Cinnamon Butter] 2. Let her smell the bowl, then the jar on its own. The jar is sharper and she will pull a face.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Cinnamon Butter] 3. Cover it and leave it on the side. Wash the fork before the mixture sets.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Swirl Buns] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Swirl Buns] 1. Flour the table and roll the dough into a rectangle the size of a tea towel. She leans on the pin, you finish the corners.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Swirl Buns] 2. She spreads the cinnamon butter to the edges with the back of a spoon. All of it goes on.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Swirl Buns] 3. Roll it up from the long side into a tight log, her hands on top of yours.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Swirl Buns] 4. Slide the floss under the log, cross the ends and pull. Cut twelve and let her count them into the tin.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Swirl Buns] 5. Space them with room to grow, cover, and leave them somewhere warm for forty minutes.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Swirl Buns] 6. Bake at 190°C for twenty-five minutes until the tops brown. Out and cooling by half eleven.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking the Round Empty-Handed] Walk the delivery round before there is anything to deliver. Stop outside each
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking the Round Empty-Handed] house, say who lives there and let her say it back and pick the door out.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking the Round Empty-Handed] February gardens give her nothing to go on, so use what stands out: the red
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking the Round Empty-Handed] door, the van, the cat in the window.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Walking the Round Empty-Handed] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Four Boxes] Twelve buns, four names, four boxes, and she decides what goes where.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Four Boxes] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Four Boxes] 1. Lay the boxes out and let her pick one for each person, saying the names aloud.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Four Boxes] 2. She colours each box for the person it is going to. The neighbour's takes twice as long.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Four Boxes] 3. Write the names big on the labels while she watches, saying the letters. She tapes each onto the right box.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Four Boxes] 4. Cut a card for each and let her make her mark inside. Ask what it says and write her words underneath.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Four Boxes] 5. Line the boxes with baking paper and load them, two buns each. She decides who gets the biggest, and the last four stay here.

D43 (Fri Feb 12) Bread for Next Door
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The oven is on and the trays come out at her head height. Say so out loud every time you open the door, and keep Kreston out of the kitchen while trays are moving. Raw dough and hot rolls go where he cannot reach them. The craft knife goes back on a high shelf between cuts.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Dough That Has to Wait] **🧰 You need:** the risen dough in its bowl · extra flour · a timer · an envelope and a pen.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Dough That Has to Wait] 1. Take the bowl out of the fridge and set it on the table in front of her. Lift the cloth and let her press one finger in. The dent stays where she put it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Dough That Has to Wait] 2. Ask how many rolls she thinks are in there. Write her number on the back of the envelope and prop it against the bowl.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Dough That Has to Wait] 3. Flour the table and set the timer for 9:15. The dough is warming up and nobody touches it until the timer goes. She will check it four times.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Rolls for the People Next Door] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Rolls for the People Next Door] 1. Tip the dough onto the floured table and let her drive both fists into it until it goes back down to the size it started. This is the loud part and it lasts as long as she wants.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Rolls for the People Next Door] 2. Cut it into twelve pieces. She rolls each one under a flat palm into a ball. The lumpy ones bake the same as the round ones.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Rolls for the People Next Door] 3. Six to a lined tray, two fingers apart, tea towel over the top. Somewhere warm for forty minutes.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Rolls for the People Next Door] 4. While they prove she draws on the two paper bags. Say the neighbours' names out loud so she knows where each bag is going.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Rolls for the People Next Door] 5. Oven at 425°F. Bake fifteen minutes, until the tops are brown and the bottom of one sounds hollow when you knock it. Out around 10:25.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Rolls for the People Next Door] 6. Three warm rolls into each bag at snack time. She eats one of the other six while she loads them.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Delivering While It's Still Warm] Coats on over the flour. Go while the paper is still warm, next door first and
    [🌳 Get Outside: Delivering While It's Still Warm] the second house after. She carries both bags herself. She knocks, she holds
    [🌳 Get Outside: Delivering While It's Still Warm] the bag out, she says who made them. February wind pulls the heat out of
    [🌳 Get Outside: Delivering While It's Still Warm] bread in about ten minutes, so knock on both doors first and do the rest of
    [🌳 Get Outside: Delivering While It's Still Warm] the walk afterwards.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Delivering While It's Still Warm] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letterbox by the Door] Everything Azlyn makes this week needs somewhere to go until Thursday. The
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letterbox by the Door] letterbox goes up by the front door this afternoon.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letterbox by the Door] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letterbox by the Door] 1. Cut the slot in the lid: a long rectangle wide enough for a folded sheet and her whole hand. Your job, with her holding the far end steady.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letterbox by the Door] 2. Tape the lid on all the way round so the slot is the only way in.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letterbox by the Door] 3. Cover the box in paper and hand her the crayons. Every side is hers. Ask what a post box needs written on it and write down her words.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letterbox by the Door] 4. Hold it against the wall by the front door and let her say when the height is right. Tape or clip it there.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letterbox by the Door] 5. First post: she draws one thing for each person in the house, folds them, posts them. Nobody looks.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Letterbox by the Door] 6. Say the rule out loud once: it stays shut until Thursday, and anything she makes for Thursday goes in it. Some things go straight to somebody's door the same day, and you say which.
D44 (Sat Feb 13) A Letter from Denmark
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Snowdrops are poisonous. The one Kreston swipes at stays in your hand, and the taped one goes inside a letter on a shelf he cannot reach. Snipped offcuts and paper confetti are mouth-sized, so sweep the floor before he is down on it, and keep the greaseproof sheet whole. Scissors go back to you between letters.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Practice Snowflakes] **🧰 You need:** white paper, 3 sheets · child-safe scissors.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Practice Snowflakes] 1. Fold a sheet in half, then in half again. Show her the folded corner and tell her that corner is the middle of the paper.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Practice Snowflakes] 2. She snips lumps out of the folded edges. Do not pencil any guidelines on it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Practice Snowflakes] 3. Open it out together. Do two more and let her work out that snipping the folded edge makes the holes. The fourth sheet is the real one and it happens at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Letter Nobody Signs] Danish children send a *gækkebrev* in the weeks before Easter: paper lace, a
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Letter Nobody Signs] rhyme, and dots instead of a name.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Letter Nobody Signs] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Letter Nobody Signs] 1. Fold the good sheet the way she practised. She snips it, you keep your hands off it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Letter Nobody Signs] 2. Open it out flat and look at it in the window before you write on it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Letter Nobody Signs] 3. Ask her what the letter should say and write her rhyme in the middle of the lace. If she stalls, offer her the shape and let her fill it: *I made this, I made this thread, guess my name and get the bread.* Take whatever she gives you.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Letter Nobody Signs] 4. Count the letters in her name on your fingers. Five. She draws five dots along the bottom of the letter.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Letter Nobody Signs] 5. Colour the lace if she wants it coloured. Fold it into the envelope and seal it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Letter Nobody Signs] 6. It goes on the walk to the neighbours who got the bread yesterday, with nobody's name on the front.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Looking for the First Snowdrops] Snowdrops are up by the middle of February, in the shade under hedges, along

    [🎨 Second Main Event: Where the Colours Run] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Where the Colours Run] 1. Set the tray on the table and lay one sheet inside it. Everything from here runs off the edges.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Where the Colours Run] 2. Thin the morning's colours with water until they drip off the brush in a stream instead of a blob.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Where the Colours Run] 3. Wet the whole sheet with the sponge until it shines, then mop the standing water off with kitchen roll.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Turmeric and paprika stain skin, grout and clothes, so keep them to paper and the old T-shirt.     [🎨 The Main Event: Painting on the Cave Wall] 2. Sponge first. Half a sponge loaded with cocoa and pressed flat gives a big soft brown mark.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Painting on the Cave Wall] 3. Now the hand. Press her palm flat on the paper, hold it there, and dab paint around the edge. Lift straight up and the shape of her hand stays behind.

    [🌳 Get Outside: Digging for Colour] ground is wet all the way through, so it gives under the blade. Look for
    [🌳 Get Outside: Digging for Colour] red-brown clay a spade down, pale chalky stone and charcoal from a cold fire
    [🌳 Get Outside: Digging for Colour] site. Rub each find on a paving slab, and whatever leaves a colour comes home
    [🌳 Get Outside: Digging for Colour] in the bag.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Digging for Colour] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Paint Made of Mud] The bag from this morning holds mud, stone and burnt wood, none of it paint
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Paint Made of Mud] yet.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Paint Made of Mud] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Paint Made of Mud] 1. Work at the sink or out on the step, because the next ninety minutes are dirty.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Paint Made of Mud] 2. One tub per material. She breaks each lump down with the back of the spoon, and it takes real effort.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Paint Made of Mud] 3. Sieve the powder into a clean tub. The fine dust is paint, and the grit left in the sieve goes in the bin.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Paint Made of Mud] 4. Add water a spoonful at a time until it pours like cream, then a spoonful of flour so it grips the paper.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Paint Made of Mud] 5. Test each tub with a stick on the box. Some give a strong colour, some a smear of wet dirt, and she sorts them into two piles.

D54 (Tue Feb 23) Over, Under, Over
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Dry pasta and cut straw segments are the right size to choke an 8-month-old, and the floor is where they land. Sweep before Kreston goes down and keep his bowl separate. No cord or beaded necklace goes on a baby, awake or asleep. Knot Azlyn's loose enough to pull over her head, take them off before swimming and before bed, and keep the wool and loose string up out of Kreston's reach all day.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Stringing the Loom] **🧰 You need:** 1 stiff cardboard rectangle · adult scissors (adult only) · wool · masking tape.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Stringing the Loom] 1. Cut notches along the top and bottom edges of the card, a finger's width apart and about a centimetre deep. You cut, she counts them out loud.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Stringing the Loom] 2. Hook the wool into the first notch, run it down to the notch opposite, round the back and up again. She holds the card steady while you wind.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Stringing the Loom] 3. Tape the loose end down at the back. It looks like a small harp now, and she can pluck it until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over and Under] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over and Under] 1. Cut the strips before she sits down: magazine, carrier bag and old T-shirt, each a finger wide and longer than the loom is across.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over and Under] 2. Wrap one end of each strip in masking tape to make a stiff point, a needle's job at a size her hand can hold.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over and Under] 3. Say the words while she does the first row. *Over, under, over, under.* Three rows of that, then go quiet.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over and Under] 4. Row two has to start under wherever row one started over. She will get this wrong, and a wrong row still looks good.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over and Under] 5. Push each finished row up tight against the one above. The gaps close and the card disappears.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Over and Under] 6. Peg the loose ends at the sides so nothing slides back out.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Fence Weave] Take a bag of long strips and go and weave the street. A chain-link fence, a
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Fence Weave] gate, a railing, a bare hedge. Feed the strips, wool and dead grass in and out
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Fence Weave] of the gaps until a patch of colour lands on a grey street. Leave it for
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Fence Weave] whoever walks past next, and come back Thursday to see whether the wind took
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Fence Weave] it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Fence Weave] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Necklace Each] The pasta came out of the freezer bags last night in colours nothing in this
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Necklace Each] kitchen has any right to be.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Necklace Each] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: One Necklace Each] 1. Tip each colour into its own bowl and let her look at the whole set before she threads anything.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Standing water and a newly crawling 8-month-old. Kreston can drown in the depth of a washing-up bowl in the time it takes to answer the door. Tip it out the moment she is finished, and never leave the room with it full.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: Ten Things That Feel Different] 2. Keep your hands out of the choosing. If she takes the emery board and leaves the ribbon in the drawer, the ribbon stays in the drawer.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Ten Things That Feel Different] 3. Sit on the floor and lay all ten in a line. Ask which is the roughest, leave that one at the end of the row, and go and have breakfast.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Touch Line] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**

    [🌳 Get Outside: Bare Hands on Cold Things] brick wall, a railing in the sun and the same railing in the shade. Metal pulls
    [🌳 Get Outside: Bare Hands on Cold Things] heat out of her hand faster than brick, so the gate feels colder than the wall
    [🌳 Get Outside: Bare Hands on Cold Things] at the same temperature and she will say so. Gloves back on the moment she says
    [🌳 Get Outside: Bare Hands on Cold Things] cold.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Bare Hands on Cold Things] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Warm Water Table] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Warm Water Table] 1. Fill the big bowl with warm water, wrist-tested, and the small one with cold. Set them a hand's width apart on the towels.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Warm Water Table] 2. Both hands into the warm bowl, count to ten together, then both hands into the cold and count again. Ask her which hand knows the difference.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Warm Water Table] 3. Put out the jug, cups, spoons, sponges and whisk, then sit down where she can see you and stop giving instructions.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Warm Water Table] 4. She will pour one bowl into the other within four minutes. Let the two temperatures become one and let her hands find out.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Warm Water Table] 5. Drop two drops of colouring into the cold bowl as interest dips. That buys you the whole second half.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Warm Water Table] 6. Hand her a sponge to soak and squeeze out over the big bowl. Heavy work for her hands, and she stays with it longest of all.
D59 (Sun Feb 28) Four Tastes
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Kreston is 8 months and gets none of this. No honey before one year, no salt, no lemonade. Cut anything he does get into strips rather than rounds, stay at his eye level while he eats, and keep grapes, whole crackers and lemon rind off the floor where he now travels.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Cutting the Tasting Plate] **🧰 You need:** 1 lemon · 1 apple · plain crackers · 1 table knife · 4 small saucers.

    [🌳 Get Outside: Hunting for the First Green] hedges, daffodil spears standing in the cold, bulbs pushing through the gap

    [🌳 Get Outside: Hunting for the First Green] fat buds on bare twigs count. Stand the best one in a cup of water on the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Hunting for the First Green] windowsill where she can watch it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Hunting for the First Green] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Fixing the Lemonade] Three lemons, a jug and a bag of sugar. She is in charge of how it tastes and
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Fixing the Lemonade] you are the one doing what she says.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Fixing the Lemonade] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Fixing the Lemonade] 1. Halve the lemons and let her squeeze them. A fork stabbed into the cut half and twisted works as well as a squeezer and takes more effort out of her arms.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Fixing the Lemonade] 2. Pour the juice through the sieve into the jug. Let her taste it neat, one teaspoon. The face from this morning comes back.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Fixing the Lemonade] 3. Add sugar a spoon at a time. She stirs, she tastes with a clean spoon, she says more or stop.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Fixing the Lemonade] 4. She will overshoot and make it too sweet to drink. Say nothing, hand her the water and let her add a splash at a time, tasting after each one, until she works out she can go too far in both directions.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Fixing the Lemonade] 5. She calls it finished. That is the recipe, and nobody adjusts it after she has said so.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Fixing the Lemonade] 6. She pours a cup for every person in the house and carries them round herself.

D61 (Tue Mar 2) Making a Face
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Salt dough is dangerous swallowed, and at 8 months Kreston puts everything in his mouth and can now reach the table leg. Give him his own sealed bag of dough to squash and nothing loose. If you cook the dough, the pan is hot and the doorway is where he will be.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Two Colours of Dough] **🧰 You need:** the dough · food colouring · 2 bowls · a floured board.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Two Colours of Dough] 1. Split the dough in half and put a few drops of colour into the middle of each lump. She pushes the colour in with her thumbs.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Two Colours of Dough] 2. Knead until the streaks disappear. It takes longer than she expects, which is the reason it sits at 8:00 and not at 9:15.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Two Colours of Dough] 3. Ask whose face she plans to make. Let her say it out loud, then leave her an hour to change her mind, because she will.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Nose That Sticks Out] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Nose That Sticks Out] 1. Prop the mirror at her eye level and let her study her own face before her hands touch the dough.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Nose That Sticks Out] 2. Roll a flat oval on the tray for the face. She does the rolling pin, you hold the tray still.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Nose That Sticks Out] 3. Eyes first, because she will do those first anyway. Two balls, pressed flat with a thumb.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Nose That Sticks Out] 4. Now the nose, and it has to come up off the tray. A flat blob will not pass. Send her back to the mirror to run one finger down the side of her own.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Nose That Sticks Out] 5. Ears next, out sideways past the edge of the oval. Have her find her own with both hands before she rolls them.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Nose That Sticks Out] 6. Hair, eyebrows and mouth last, with the fork dragged through for texture and the knife for the mouth line. Photograph it, because a dough face does not survive the day.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Faces on the Ground] Take a bag and collect face parts off the ground: two matching stones for eyes,
    [🌳 Get Outside: Faces on the Ground] a stick for a mouth, a curl of bark for a nose, moss or dead grass for hair.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Faces on the Ground] Build the face on a flat bit of pavement or on a tree stump and leave it there
    [🌳 Get Outside: Faces on the Ground] for whoever comes next. Photograph it and check tomorrow whether it survived.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Faces on the Ground] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: A Family That Stands Up] Everyone in the house gets made out of dough, and every one of them has to stand
    [🎨 Second Main Event: A Family That Stands Up] on the tray without falling over.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: A Family That Stands Up] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: A Family That Stands Up] 1. Prop your phone up with the family photo on it where she can see it while she works.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: A Family That Stands Up] 2. Start with the smallest. Kreston is a body, a head and no legs worth speaking of, so he stands up on the first try.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: A Family That Stands Up] 3. Azlyn next. She rolls two legs and tests them on the tray before the head goes on top.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: A Family That Stands Up] 4. One of them will topple. Ask her what would fix it and wait. Thicker legs, bigger feet or a tube behind the back are all fair answers, and she picks.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: A Family That Stands Up] 5. Build the tall ones last. They fall over most, and by then she has three collapses behind her to work from.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: A Family That Stands Up] 6. Stand the whole family on the tray in height order, measure them against the ruler, and photograph the row beside the real family photo so she can tell you who is who.
D62 (Wed Mar 3) What Ears Are For
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Take the string telephone down and put the string away the second you finish. A loop of string at toddler height is a strangulation risk, and Kreston crawls now, so it never stays up while you make a cup of tea. Scissors and the skewer stay with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Paper Cones] **🧰 You need:** 4 sheets of paper or thin card · masking tape · 2 elastic bands.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Paper Cones] 1. Roll one sheet into a cone with a wide mouth and a hole at the point big enough for her ear. Hold it closed while she winds the elastic band on.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Paper Cones] 2. She rolls the next two herself, one fat and one thin. Crooked is fine, and turns out to be useful.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Paper Cones] 3. Hold each one to her ear and say nothing about which sounds best. She finds that out at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ear Trumpet Test] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ear Trumpet Test] 1. Send the noise away first. A ticking clock in the next room, or the saucepan and spoon at the far end of the hall.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ear Trumpet Test] 2. Bare ears first. Let her tell you how much she gets from where she stands.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ear Trumpet Test] 3. Cone one against one ear, other ear covered with her hand. Same sound, same distance. Ask her better or the same.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ear Trumpet Test] 4. Cone two, then cone three, in that order, at that distance, with nothing else changed between goes.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ear Trumpet Test] 5. She calls the winner, even where you would have picked another. Tape that cone so it holds its shape.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Ear Trumpet Test] 6. She decorates the winner with crayons and it is hers for the day. The other two go on the shelf until 4:15, and she says what happens to them.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Listening Stop] Walk to a bench or a low wall and stop dead for two minutes. Nobody talks. Hold
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Listening Stop] up a finger for each sound she catches: a car, a crow, a door, a plane, wind in
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Listening Stop] a bare hedge, her own breathing. Early March carries sound further than summer
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Listening Stop] does, with no leaves out yet to soak it up. Take the trumpet and let her aim it
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Listening Stop] at the furthest one.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Listening Stop] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The String Telephone] Two cups and five metres of string between two rooms. She works out the rule
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The String Telephone] before you get to say it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The String Telephone] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The String Telephone] 1. Pierce one small hole in the bottom of each cup yourself, dead centre, with her hands well back.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The String Telephone] 2. Thread the string in from the outside and knot it round a paperclip inside the cup so it cannot pull back through. She holds the cup while you tie.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The String Telephone] 3. Test it across one room, both of you standing, string pulled tight. You talk, she listens, then swap over.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The String Telephone] 4. Let the string go slack and try again. She gets nothing, and she will tell you before you ask. Run it round a door frame into the next room and it dies at the corner too. Leave her to find the straight line that brings it back.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The String Telephone] 5. Whisper down it. That is the run that convinces her, because she can see your mouth moving and hears nothing without the cup at her ear.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The String Telephone] 6. Take it down, wind the string up and put it away before you do anything else.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Spring collecting means bulbs, berries and yew within her reach. Daffodil bulbs are poisonous and so is yew, and both are out on the verges this month.     [🌅 Opening Activity: Twelve Empty Cups] 3. Give her the rule now: twelve cups, twelve things, no two the same. Leave her the rest of the hour to decide where she wants to look first.

    [🌳 Get Outside: Catkins and Sticky Buds] yellow tails and give off a puff of pollen when she flicks one. Find a horse

    [🌳 Get Outside: Catkins and Sticky Buds] varnish. Break one twig off to stand in water at home. She watches it open
    [🌳 Get Outside: Catkins and Sticky Buds] indoors before the tree outside does.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: Labels for Everything] your way, then the whole lot goes on a board that hangs up for the week.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Labels for Everything] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**

D89 (Tue Mar 30) Sink, Float, Spin
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Raw egg goes on her hands, the tray and the worktop. Wash with soap before anyone touches Kreston, the snack or a tap handle.     [🌅 Opening Activity: Guessing Before the Water] 1. Line the objects up on the table with two bowls behind them, one marked SINK and one FLOAT.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: Guessing Before the Water] 3. No testing yet. The bowls stay as she leaves them until the water arrives at 9:15.

    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg That Learns to Float] 2. Fill both jars two-thirds with warm water. Lower an egg into the first with the spoon. It sits on the bottom, which she predicted.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg That Learns to Float] 3. One tablespoon of salt at a time into the second jar, stirred until the water goes clear again. She counts the spoons.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg That Learns to Float] 4. Lower the second egg in around spoon five. Somewhere near eight it lifts off the bottom and rides at the top with a coin of shell showing.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg That Learns to Float] 5. Ask her what she thinks is holding it up. Write her answer word for word on a scrap and tape it to the glass.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Egg That Learns to Float] 6. Trickle plain water down the back of the spoon into the salty jar. The egg rises to the join and sits there, halfway up the glass, and she will make you look at it all week.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Races in the Gutter] March rain leaves the gutters running for hours. Drop two sticks in at the top
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Races in the Gutter] of the hill and walk down beside them, and the one she picked will lose. Find
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Races in the Gutter] where the water speeds up and where it pools behind a drain grate. Poke the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Races in the Gutter] leaves clear and watch what changes, and put her wellies on before you open the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Races in the Gutter] door.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Stick Races in the Gutter] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

    [🎨 Second Main Event: Raw Egg, Cooked Egg] three ways without cracking one open.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Raw Egg, Cooked Egg] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Raw Egg, Cooked Egg] 1. Mark the four eggs A, B, C and D. Tell her two are cooked and let her guess which before she touches anything.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Raw Egg, Cooked Egg] 2. Spin each one flat on the tray. The cooked ones go like a top. The raw ones wobble and stall, because the liquid inside keeps moving after the shell stops.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Raw Egg, Cooked Egg] 3. Spin one, stop it with a finger for half a second, then let go. A raw egg starts turning again on its own. A cooked one stays dead.

    [🎨 The Main Event: The Penguin Shuffle] 5. The hand-off: she shuffles the egg onto your feet and you shuffle it back, hands behind your backs. Real penguins lose eggs here too.

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Waddle to the Corner] high. Pigeon: head forward and back with every step, which she does better than
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Waddle to the Corner] you. Let her call the changes.
