D101 (Sun Apr 11) What the Stem Drinks
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The knife is adult-only and goes back out of reach between cuts. If you use a carnation or a daisy instead of celery, the cut stems stay on the table and off the floor. Kreston will drink the coloured water if he reaches it, so keep the jars back from the edge.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Three Jars of Colour] **🧰 You need:** 3 jars · food colouring, blue and red · 1 sheet of paper · 1 marker.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Three Jars of Colour] 1. She fills all three jars two thirds full at the tap and carries them over one at a time.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Three Jars of Colour] 2. Blue in the first, red in the second, nothing in the third. Ten drops each, counted by her, and she swirls them to mix.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Three Jars of Colour] 3. Ask what each jar would do to a stick of celery. Write her three answers on the paper, her words.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Celery That Turns Blue] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Celery That Turns Blue] 1. Slice a thin piece off the bottom of each stalk with the table knife while she watches, and stand each in a jar while the cut is fresh. The knife goes back out of reach.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Celery That Turns Blue] 2. One stalk per jar, leaves standing clear of the water. The plain jar proves the other two, so it gets a stalk as good as theirs.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Celery That Turns Blue] 3. Top all three up so every stem stands in the same depth.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Celery That Turns Blue] 4. Read her predictions back and let her change any now the stalks are in. Write the new version under the old and leave both.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Celery That Turns Blue] 5. Set the jars on the windowsill where she can see without lifting them. Nothing shows for an hour and a half.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Celery That Turns Blue] 6. Check at snack, at lunch and at bedtime. Colour reaches the stem first and the leaves last, so tonight is the payoff.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ribbon on the Bud] Every tree on the street is opening this week. Pick one branch at her height,
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ribbon on the Bud] tie a strip of wool round a single closed bud, and look at that bud and
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ribbon on the Bud] nothing else. Come back to it on every walk from here. Indoors the jars are
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ribbon on the Bud] doing the same business faster.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Ribbon on the Bud] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] Celery has a bundle of pipes running up the inside. Azlyn builds one, drinks
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] through it, then finds the same thing in her lunch.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] 1. She gathers the straws into a fist and holds them level while you tape the bundle top and bottom.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] 2. Fill the glass and let her drink through the whole bundle. It works, and it takes some doing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] 3. Pull one straw out and let her drink through that alone. Ask which moved more water and let her answer stand.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] 4. Lift a stalk out of the blue jar onto the white plate and cut it straight across the middle yourself. The knife goes back out of reach.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] 5. She counts the blue dots in the cut face. Every dot is a pipe.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] 6. Cut a second slice near the leaves and hold the two faces side by side to see how far the colour climbed.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Bundle of Straws] 7. She draws the cut face with crayons: the stalk outline and one dot for every pipe she finds.
D102 (Mon Apr 12) The Wrong Garden
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The coin, the button, the raisin and the dry pasta all fit down a 10-month-old's throat, so the tray stays up on the table and the planted cups stand at the back of the sill, out of reach of a boy who cruises the furniture. The stapler and any loose staples go back in the drawer the moment the booklet is made.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Could This Grow?] **🧰 You need:** 1 tray · 10 small things from round the house · 2 apple pips.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Could This Grow?] 1. Load the tray while she watches: the apple pips, a raisin, a button, a coin, a leaf, a stone, dry pasta, a sock, a fork and a toy car.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Could This Grow?] 2. Hold up one thing at a time and ask the same question. Could this grow? Two piles, yes and no, and her answer decides. Leave the raisin where she puts it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Could This Grow?] 3. Ask how a person would find out for certain. Tell her the no pile gets planted at 9:15, and keep your face straight.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Garden of Silly Things] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Garden of Silly Things] 1. She scrunches newspaper into six of the cups until it packs firm, or fills them with dry soil if you would rather have the mess than the noise. The seventh gets a scoop of real soil.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Garden of Silly Things] 2. One silly object per newspaper cup. The fork stands up, the sock goes in loose, the raisin gets buried, and she decides how deep each one goes. The two apple pips go in the soil cup, the one honest answer standing in the row.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Garden of Silly Things] 3. Every cup gets a label. She draws what is coming up on a lolly stick or a strip of card, taped to the rim of its own cup.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Garden of Silly Things] 4. Write her words under each drawing with the marker, as she says them. A fork tree. A car with wheels growing on it. Do not improve the grammar.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Garden of Silly Things] 5. Stand the row at the back of the windowsill beside the celery jars and let her set the order.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Garden of Silly Things] 6. Ask which comes up first. Write her answer on the last label and read the whole row back before you leave it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Naming the Weeds Wrong] The verges are full this week and none of it was planted. Dandelions, daisies,
    [🌳 Get Outside: Naming the Weeds Wrong] buttercups and the first cow parsley are up along the walls. Name each one
    [🌳 Get Outside: Naming the Weeds Wrong] wrong on purpose as you pass: that is a spaghetti bush, those are elephant
    [🌳 Get Outside: Naming the Weeds Wrong] toes. She will correct you, then start naming them herself.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Naming the Weeds Wrong] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Story of What Came Up] Something happened in the silly garden while everyone was out, and Azlyn is
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Story of What Came Up] the only person who knows what.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Story of What Came Up] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Story of What Came Up] 1. Stack the four sheets, fold them in half together and staple along the fold, or run masking tape down it. Eight pages, and she holds it while you make it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Story of What Came Up] 2. Bring the cups off the windowsill and set them in front of her in a row. Start with the fork. Ask what came up.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Story of What Came Up] 3. Write what she says in pen, word for word, one sentence to a page. Her grammar stays as it arrives.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Story of What Came Up] 4. Work along all seven cups. If she dries up, ask what the sock did in the night, or who else in the house saw it happen.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Story of What Came Up] 5. She illustrates every page in crayon, or does all the drawing in one go at the end.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Story of What Came Up] 6. Front cover last: her title, her name and the date, with you writing what she dictates and nothing else.

D103 (Tue Apr 13) Azlyn's Round
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Stand the stool on carpet, never on tile, and keep a hand on her the whole time she is up on it. The blanket comes off her face the moment she asks. At the pool hold Kreston or sit him in a seat, never down on wet tile, and stay within arm's reach of him in the water.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Everyone Who Needs Something] **🧰 You need:** the clipboard · 1 pencil · a walk round the house.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Everyone Who Needs Something] 1. Take her round with the clipboard under her arm and ask the same question at every stop. Does this one need something today?
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Everyone Who Needs Something] 2. She draws whoever makes the list: the worm jar, the celery, the pots on the sill, the birds outside, Kreston, you. One line each, no help.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Everyone Who Needs Something] 3. She numbers the order she wants, and you follow it at 9:15 even where it makes no sense.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Azlyn's Watering Round] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Azlyn's Watering Round] 1. Fill the jug half full at the tap. A full one floods the first pot and runs dry by the fourth.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Azlyn's Watering Round] 2. Teach the finger test once, then leave it alone. She pushes a finger into the soil to the first knuckle: dry means water, damp means walk on.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Azlyn's Watering Round] 3. Water goes onto the soil and never over the leaves, and stops when the first drop runs out of the bottom.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Azlyn's Watering Round] 4. A sticker on the list for every one she finishes, or a pencil tick. The worm jar gets a look through the glass and a splash on the leaves at the top.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Azlyn's Watering Round] 5. Kreston is on the round. She fills his cup from the jug and hands it to him. You are on the list too, so the last of the jug is yours.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Azlyn's Watering Round] 6. Tea towel over every spill as she goes. The round is hers and the mopping comes with it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Route, Backwards] Walk your usual block in reverse. Start at the end you normally finish at and
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Route, Backwards] go round the wrong way. Inside two minutes she will notice that the postbox
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Route, Backwards] now comes before the red door. Stop at the ribboned bud from Sunday on the way
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Route, Backwards] past.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Route, Backwards] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] Azlyn has spent four days watching plants do something too slow to see. This
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] afternoon she is the plant, at her own speed.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] 1. She curls up as small as she goes on the floor and you lay the blanket over her. That is the seed, and it is dark under there.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] 2. Narrate the order and give her the weather. Rain first, a puff of the spray bottle over the blanket or a dab from the wet flannel. Then sun, the torch above her.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] 3. She pushes the blanket off and comes up: knees, then feet, then onto the stool with your hand steady, arms wide. Music on for the growing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] 4. Hold her at full height for a slow count of five, then run the whole thing backwards. Off the stool, standing, knees, curled, blanket over, torch off.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] 5. Do it again with her calling the order out. She will change it, and you follow the changed version.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] 6. Swap over. She works the torch and the spray and narrates, and you are the seed, folded under the blanket doing what you are told.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Seed to Sunflower and Back] 7. Finish on the floor with the torch off and the blanket over both of you.
D104 (Wed Apr 14) The Weather Station
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The craft knife is adult-only, and a cut bottle edge is sharp enough to slice a hand, so tape the rim before either child touches it. The cap and the offcuts go in the bin, because both fit down a 10-month-old's throat. Tie the cane where it cannot fall on anyone. The knife stays with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Sky at Eight] **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of paper · 1 marker · the window with the most sky.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Sky at Eight] 1. Stand at the window with her and look up for a full minute, saying nothing.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Sky at Eight] 2. She picks one word for what she sees: blue, white, grey, moving, cross. Write it at the top of the paper with the date.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Sky at Eight] 3. Ask what the sky will do by teatime and write that underneath. The sheet becomes the chart at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Weather Station by the Door] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Weather Station by the Door] 1. Cut the top third off the bottle yourself with her standing well back, then tape the cut rim twice round.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Weather Station by the Door] 2. Take the cap off and put it straight in the bin. Drop the stones in the base so it cannot blow over, sit the cut top upside down inside as a funnel, and tape them together. She marks a line up the side.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Weather Station by the Door] 3. Cut the bottom off the bag so it opens into a tube. She holds it while you tape three strings round the mouth, then knot the ends to the cane.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Weather Station by the Door] 4. She sites both. The gauge goes somewhere flat and open, clear of the guttering and the hedge. The cane goes where she can see it, tied so it cannot come down.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Weather Station by the Door] 5. Rule the chart into seven boxes, one a day. Today's gets the date and her sky word from eight o'clock.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Weather Station by the Door] 6. Stand at the window together and look at the pair of them, first thing every morning from now on.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Chalk Round the Puddles] April leaves puddles everywhere and takes them back within the day. Find a big
    [🌳 Get Outside: Chalk Round the Puddles] one on flat pavement, draw round its edge in chalk, then walk on. Come back
    [🌳 Get Outside: Chalk Round the Puddles] past it before lunch: the water has pulled in from the chalk line.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Chalk Round the Puddles] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] The gauge outside waits on real weather. This one makes rain on the kitchen
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] table in a minute and a half.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] 1. She fills the jar three quarters with water and leaves it until it goes still.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] 2. Lay the shaving foam across the top with the teaspoon, two fingers thick and flat. That is the cloud.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] 3. Spoon blue colouring onto the cloud, a little at a time. Take her guess before each one lands: is this the spoonful that rains?
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] 4. Nothing happens for a while. The foam holds the colour until it cannot, and a blue thread drops through the water to the bottom.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] 5. Let her carry on until the whole jar has gone blue and the cloud collapses. Tea towel to hand, because this one goes over the edge.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] 6. Ask what the cloud outside is doing, and go and look.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Rain in a Jar] 7. She fills in today's box on the chart with the crayon: what the sky did, and whether the gauge caught anything.
D105 (Thu Apr 15) Seeds That Fly
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** She will suck instead of blow at least once, so use washable non-toxic paint and keep the straws short, which makes sucking hard and blowing easy.     [🌅 Opening Activity: Picking the Dandelion Clocks] 3. Carry the rest home upright and out of the wind. The plan is to beat that seed at 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Seeds Can Fly] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Seeds Can Fly] 1. Blow one clock over the table and watch a single seed down to the floor. That is the thing to beat.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Seeds Can Fly] 2. Make the helicopter. Cut a strip of paper the length of your hand and two fingers wide, cut halfway down the middle, fold the two flaps opposite ways and slide the paper clip onto the bottom. Scissors away.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Seeds Can Fly] 3. Make the parachute. You cut a square of tissue paper, she tapes a thread to each corner, and the four loose ends get taped to a dried bean.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Seeds Can Fly] 4. The third one is a bean with nothing on it. She will tell you that is not fair. Agree with her and drop it anyway.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Seeds Can Fly] 5. She stands on the dining chair with your hand at her back and drops all three from the same height, then two together.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Which Seeds Can Fly] 6. Ask what the slow ones have that the bean has not. Take her answer, then look at one dandelion seed up close.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Wind Takes Them] Take the rest of the clocks out to open ground. She blows one crouched at
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Wind Takes Them] grass level, then another standing on a wall with your hand on her, and you
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Wind Takes Them] chase both sets to see which went further. April wind changes every few
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Wind Takes Them] minutes, so try again facing the other way.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Where the Wind Takes Them] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] Paint moves when she blows on it. By half past two there is a metre of meadow
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] on the floor.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] 1. Tape the paper down at all four corners and stand the tray beside it with the paint on it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] 2. Old shirt on, sleeves rolled, wet flannel in its bowl at her elbow before any paint moves.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] 3. A teaspoon of thinned green goes at the bottom edge, one puddle. She puts a short straw close to it and blows the puddle up the page.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] 4. The paint runs into thin legs and splits as it goes, which is what makes it grass. Give her the spoon for the next puddle.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] 5. Work along the bottom edge, puddle by puddle, until the grass runs across. Yellow puddles blown from halfway up make the thinner stems behind.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] 6. She fingerprints a yellow seed head on every green stem, hands to the flannel between colours.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Grass Blown Through a Straw] 7. Leave it flat to dry and hang it in the hall in the morning.

D122 (Sun May 2) The Same Square of Grass
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A bowl of water at floor height and a 10 month old who pulls up on furniture. Two inches is enough to drown in. The bowl goes on the table or stays within your arm's reach, and it gets tipped out the moment she is finished. The scissors stay with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One Sheet, Eight Pages] **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of A4 paper · 1 pair of adult scissors · 1 pencil.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One Sheet, Eight Pages] 1. Fold the sheet in half three times, letting her press each crease with her thumb. Open it flat and count the eight boxes.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One Sheet, Eight Pages] 2. Fold it back in half the long way and cut along the centre crease to the middle. You cut, she holds. Push the ends together and it opens into a book.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: One Sheet, Eight Pages] 3. She writes her name on the front in pencil. Everything this week goes in here.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Sticks and a Piece of String] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Sticks and a Piece of String] 1. Let her choose the square: somewhere she can kneel, that nobody is mowing, and that she can see from the door.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Sticks and a Piece of String] 2. Push the four sticks into the ground in a square, an arm's length a side. Tape round the top of each so they show from the house.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Sticks and a Piece of String] 3. Run the string round all four and hold it with a peg at each stick.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Sticks and a Piece of String] 4. Both of you down on your fronts. Count out loud what is in there: flowers, different leaves, anything moving. Stand the jam jar of water in front of something small and look through the glass.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Sticks and a Piece of String] 5. Page one of the notebook. She draws what is inside with the crayons, you write the date and her words underneath in pencil.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Four Sticks and a Piece of String] 6. Nothing else goes on page one. She draws the same square on Tuesday and Thursday, on pages two and three.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Pavement Crack Flowers] Walk the street with your eyes down. Dandelions, groundsel, moss and the odd
    [🌳 Get Outside: Pavement Crack Flowers] violet come up through cracks in the pavement and out of the joint where the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Pavement Crack Flowers] wall meets the path. Ask her how a flower gets into a crack with no soil in
    [🌳 Get Outside: Pavement Crack Flowers] it. Count how many you pass before the corner, and pick none of them.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Pavement Crack Flowers] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] Paper takes on water and swells along its fibres, enough to push a folded
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] petal open with nobody touching it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] 1. Draw a flower on a sheet with a crayon: a middle and six fat petals. Cut it out with the adult scissors while she holds the sheet.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] 2. She colours it, both sides. The more crayon goes on, the longer the paper takes to soak.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] 3. Fold each petal in to the middle, one lapping over the next, until it makes a bud in her palm.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] 4. Fill the bowl with two inches of water and stand it in the middle of the table.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] 5. Lay the bud on the surface, folds up, and take her hands away. Count out loud. It opens on its own inside thirty seconds.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] 6. Do the rest, changing one thing each time: more crayon, tighter folds, a bigger flower. Ask her which will be slowest.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Flower That Opens on the Water] 7. Tip the water out the moment she is finished and dry the table with the towel.
D123 (Mon May 3) Food for the Bees
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Hands washed after soil and before food, both children. Compost and garden soil carry bacteria and neither of them should be mouthing it. Bees get watched and never touched or trapped, and nobody swings at one.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sieving the Soil] **🧰 You need:** 2 cups garden soil or compost · 1 sieve · 1 large bowl · newspaper.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sieving the Soil] 1. Spread the newspaper over the table and stand the bowl in the middle of it.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sieving the Soil] 2. Hand her the sieve and let her shake soil through by the handful. Stones and roots stay on top.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Sieving the Soil] 3. Look at what stayed in the sieve before you tip it back outside. Hands away from her face until they are washed.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Seed Balls for the Bare Patch] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: Seed Balls for the Bare Patch] 1. Newspaper still down, sieved soil in the bowl. Sieve another scoop if there is less than two cups in there.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Seed Balls for the Bare Patch] 2. Add the cup of flour on top and let her mix the two dry with both hands until the colour is even.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Seed Balls for the Bare Patch] 3. Water from the jug, a splash at a time. Stop when a squeezed handful holds its shape. Too wet, and a handful more soil brings it back.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Seed Balls for the Bare Patch] 4. Tip the seed in and fold it through with her hands so it runs all the way through.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Seed Balls for the Bare Patch] 5. Roll balls the size of a walnut between her palms and line them up on the baking tray. Aim for twenty.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Seed Balls for the Bare Patch] 6. Carry five out to the bare corner by the door and press them in with her thumb while they are soft. The rest dry on the tray, somewhere warm and out of reach.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Following One Bumblebee] Bumblebees are heavy, loud and slow enough to follow on foot. The first week of
    [🌳 Get Outside: Following One Bumblebee] May is the best of the year for it, because the dandelions are at their peak
    [🌳 Get Outside: Following One Bumblebee] and the queens are out working them. Pick one working the dandelions
    [🌳 Get Outside: Following One Bumblebee] and walk with it. Count the flowers it lands on before it goes over a fence,
    [🌳 Get Outside: Following One Bumblebee] and watch the yellow build up on its back legs. Stay an arm's length back the
    [🌳 Get Outside: Following One Bumblebee] whole time.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Following One Bumblebee] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Carrying the Pollen] Pollen moves because it sticks to a fuzzy body. The whole business runs on a
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Carrying the Pollen] cotton wool ball and two spoons of flour.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Carrying the Pollen] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Carrying the Pollen] 1. Open the six cupcake cases out and let her colour the insides with the crayons, one colour each.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Carrying the Pollen] 2. Tape each case down onto the tray, spread apart, so they stay put when she lands on them.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Carrying the Pollen] 3. Tape the cotton wool ball to the peg. She holds the peg, and the cotton wool is the fuzzy back of the bee.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Carrying the Pollen] 4. Brush a pinch of flour into two of the six cases with the small paintbrush. The other four stay clean, and she checks that.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Carrying the Pollen] 5. Fly the bee into a floury flower and press it down. Look at the cotton wool, then fly to a clean flower and press again.
D124 (Tue May 4) The Snails Get Names
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Snails and slugs carry parasites and soil bacteria. Hands scrubbed after handling and before any food, and nothing goes near a face. Snails, small stones and short sticks are all mouth-sized for Kreston at 10 months, so the tray stays up on the table. The scissors stay with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Little Rooms] **🧰 You need:** 3 strips of corrugated card · 3 cardboard tubes · parcel tape.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Little Rooms] 1. Roll one strip into a spiral so the ridges make tunnels, then let her roll the other two. Hers come out loose, which is better.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Little Rooms] 2. Push a spiral into each tube. If one springs out, wrap tape round the roll first and try again.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Rolling the Little Rooms] 3. Hold a tube up to her eye and let her look down it. The gaps are the rooms.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bug Hotel by the Fence] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bug Hotel by the Fence] 1. Take the lid off the shoebox and stand the box on its long side so the opening faces you. The lid goes underneath.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bug Hotel by the Fence] 2. Cut the tubes down with the scissors so they finish flush with the front. You cut, she holds each one against the box.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bug Hotel by the Fence] 3. Pack the tubes in on their sides until nothing rattles. Roll the fourth strip of card into a spiral and wedge it in a corner.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bug Hotel by the Fence] 4. Push short sticks in on end to fill the gaps, then press dry grass into what is left. Nothing should fall out when she tips it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bug Hotel by the Fence] 5. Run parcel tape round the box twice, or tie string round it both ways, so it holds once it has been rained on.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Bug Hotel by the Fence] 6. Wedge it against the fence, opening away from the weather and off the wet ground if you can. Brick on top.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Turning Over the Stones] Every flat stone, log and upturned pot has a colony under it. Lift, look, and
    [🌳 Get Outside: Turning Over the Stones] put it back the same way up, because it is somebody's roof. Woodlice, worms,
    [🌳 Get Outside: Turning Over the Stones] centipedes, and on a damp May morning, snails. Take two or three snails and a
    [🌳 Get Outside: Turning Over the Stones] leaf indoors in a tub for this afternoon, and chalk a cross on the stone each
    [🌳 Get Outside: Turning Over the Stones] one came off.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Turning Over the Stones] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Two Snails and a Wet Tray] Two snails from the morning hunt get an afternoon as guests.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Two Snails and a Wet Tray] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Two Snails and a Wet Tray] 1. Wet the tray all over with the cloth. A dry surface is no good to a snail.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Two Snails and a Wet Tray] 2. Lift each snail on with the soft paintbrush, under the foot and never by the shell. Never pull a snail off a surface it has gripped.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Two Snails and a Wet Tray] 3. She names them. Write the names on the paper and let her draw each shell in crayon, spiral and all.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Two Snails and a Wet Tray] 4. Set the leaf at one end and sit back while they find it. It takes longer than she expects.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Two Snails and a Wet Tray] 5. Outside, chalk a start and a finish line on a paving slab, a hand's width apart, and wipe the slab damp.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Two Snails and a Wet Tray] 6. Lift two snails to the start with the brush, put the leaf past the finish, hands off. The winner wins nothing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Two Snails and a Wet Tray] 7. Back on the wet tray, then back to their own chalked stones before dinner. Wash hands, and leave the notebook and the crayons out, because she draws page two of the pegged square at 4:15.
D125 (Wed May 5) The Big Bug Count
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Long grass in May means ticks, so check both children's hairlines, necks and behind the knees when you come in. Nettles are up and sting on contact. Bees and wasps get looked at through the jar and never picked up.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Six Boxes and a Picture Each] **🧰 You need:** 1 sheet of paper · 1 piece of stiff card · 1 ruler · 1 pencil · crayons · masking tape.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Six Boxes and a Picture Each] 1. Tape the sheet of paper along its top edge onto the stiff card. That is her clipboard.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Six Boxes and a Picture Each] 2. Rule five lines down the page so it comes out in six columns. Her hand on the ruler, yours on the pencil.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Six Boxes and a Picture Each] 3. She draws one creature at the top of each column in crayon: ant, woodlouse, snail, bee, spider, worm.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Big Bug Count] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Big Bug Count] 1. Sun hat on, clipboard under her arm, one pencil in her hand and the spare in your pocket, because the first gets dropped.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Big Bug Count] 2. Show her the mark once. One line for one creature, in the column under its picture.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Big Bug Count] 3. Start at the front step and work round the house in one direction. Under stones, up the wall, in the long grass.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Big Bug Count] 4. For anything worth a longer look, put the clear cup over it and slide the paper lid underneath. Stand the jam jar of water beside it and look through both.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Big Bug Count] 5. Anything that needs moving gets moved with the soft paintbrush. Fingers stay off, and whatever came out of a stone goes back under it.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Big Bug Count] 6. Come in when the page is full or she is finished, and leave every mark as she made it.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Same Count on the Green] Take the clipboard and the spare pencil to the green at the end of the road
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Same Count on the Green] and run the count again on ground nobody looks after. Different grass,
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Same Count on the Green] different bugs. The numbers are in the long stuff, so wade in with her rather
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Same Count on the Green] than working from the path.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Same Count on the Green] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] Her tally sheet answers a question she cannot read off it yet. Stuck up in
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] columns, the answer is the tallest one.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] 1. Big sheet on the table the long way round. Rule a line along the bottom with the ruler and mark six spaces, one per bug.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] 2. She draws the same six creatures along the bottom in crayon, copying her own pictures off the morning sheet.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] 3. Take the first column of tallies. Count the marks out loud and let her stick that many labels in a stack going up from her drawing.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] 4. Do the other five the same way. She sticks, you count and hand them over, and every square sits on top of the last.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] 5. Stand back and look at it from the door. The tallest stack is the winner.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] 6. Ask which one she thought would win before she started, and write her answer along the top.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Turning the Marks into a Picture] 7. Glue the morning's tally sheet into the bottom corner so both live on one page, and put it on the wall at her height.
D126 (Thu May 6) A Garden in a Jar
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A big glass jar full of wet soil is heavy, and Kreston is 11 months and standing. It lives at the back of a high sill, never on a low table or a sill he can pull up to. Hands washed after the soil, both children. The scissors stay with you.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Scrubbing the Pebbles] **🧰 You need:** a handful of pebbles or gravel · 1 bowl of warm water · 1 old toothbrush · 1 tea towel.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Scrubbing the Pebbles] 1. Pebbles into the bowl and let her scrub them with the toothbrush. The water goes brown fast.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Scrubbing the Pebbles] 2. Line the clean ones up on the tea towel to dry, biggest to smallest if she wants an order.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Scrubbing the Pebbles] 3. Tell her what they are for: spare water sits in the bottom of the jar and not in the roots.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Jar That Waters Itself] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Jar That Waters Itself] 1. Tip the scrubbed pebbles into the jar, two fingers deep, and let her shake it flat.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Jar That Waters Itself] 2. Spoon in two cups of soil and press it down with the back of the spoon.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Jar That Waters Itself] 3. Dig a hole with the spoon and stand the plant or the cuttings in it. She firms the soil round the stems with her thumbs.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Jar That Waters Itself] 4. Press the moss down over the bare soil like a carpet, green side up, out to the glass.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Jar That Waters Itself] 5. Two spoonfuls of water from the jug and no more. Damp, not wet. Wipe the inside of the glass with the cloth so she can see in.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Jar That Waters Itself] 6. Lid on tight. She writes the date on the label in marker and it goes on the front. Stand it at the back of a bright sill, out of direct sun.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Moss Hunt] Moss grows where nothing else gets a hold: north-facing walls, the shady side
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Moss Hunt] of the shed, the joint between two paving slabs. Take a spoon and a tub and
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Moss Hunt] collect three kinds. One will be flat, one will look like a forest half an inch
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Moss Hunt] high, and one comes up in cushions.
    [🌳 Get Outside: The Moss Hunt] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] Sealed between two strips of tape, a flower keeps its colour and the light
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] comes through it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] 1. Pull an arm's length of packing tape off the roll and lay it sticky side up. Two short tabs off the roll hold the ends down.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] 2. She lays the pressed flowers along it face down and spaced out. Anything with a lump in it will not seal.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] 3. Lay a second strip of tape over the top, sticky side down, and press the air out from the middle with her thumb.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] 4. Peel the whole strip up off the table. Hold it against the window and look at what the light does.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] 5. Fold thin card in half, pencil a window on the front, and cut through both layers with the adult scissors.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] 6. Tape the strip inside the fold so it shows through, and let her write on the frame in pencil.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Flowers Between Two Pieces of Tape] 7. Tape a loop of string to the back and hang it against the glass. Leave the notebook by the back door for 4:15: she draws page three kneeling at the pegged square, then the three pages go side by side on the table with Sunday's on the left.
D127 (Fri May 7) The Family Photographer
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Peel the six floor tapes up the moment you finish and count them. Masking tape is exactly what an 11-month-old puts in his mouth. The camera strap goes round her wrist, never her neck, and comes off before she runs.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Cardboard Viewfinder] **🧰 You need:** the cardboard frame from last night · masking tape · 1 permanent marker.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Cardboard Viewfinder] 1. She holds the frame up to one eye and walks the room with it. Ask her what is inside the window and what got left out.

    [🎨 The Main Event: Her Turn With the Camera] 4. After each one, ask why that. Write her answer on the numbered line word for word, wrong grammar and all.

    [🌳 Get Outside: Birds Carrying Breakfast] Every bird on the street is running a delivery service this week. Stand still by
    [🌳 Get Outside: Birds Carrying Breakfast] a hedge and watch for one flying with a full beak, a grub or a worm or a lump of
    [🌳 Get Outside: Birds Carrying Breakfast] moss, and it never stops to eat any of it. Follow it and you find the nest. Take
    [🌳 Get Outside: Birds Carrying Breakfast] the camera and let her photograph the tree, even with no bird in it.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] Six spots in the house, three heights at each, eighteen photographs. Kreston
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] lives in a different version of this room.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] 1. Walk the downstairs together and pick six spots: the kitchen doorway, under the table, the bottom stair, wherever she says. Press a piece of masking tape on the floor at each and number them one to six in marker.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] 2. Sit Kreston's toy on the floor at spot one. Kneel down and hold the camera at the height of his head for the first photograph.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] 3. Same tape, same spot, and she takes the second one standing at her own eye height.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] 4. For the third she stretches the camera as high as her arms go, or you lift her up and she shoots from there.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] 5. Work through all six spots that way. Tick each height off on the paper as you go, because eighteen photographs run together fast.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] 6. Sit down with the screen and give her three shots of one spot out of order. Let her say whose is whose before you tell her.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The World From Kreston's Height] 7. Hunt for the toy in all three. It fills the frame from Kreston's height and you can hardly find it from yours.

D129 (Sun May 9) Mother's Day
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Cow parsley on a verge has two dangerous lookalikes. Hemlock has a smooth purple-blotched stem, giant hogweed is head-high with a thick bristly stem, and hogweed sap burns skin in sunlight for days. D134 (Fri May 14) Bubbles and Kites
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Soapy water turns paving into a skating rink, so keep the towel down, run the big string loop out on the grass, and rinse the slabs afterwards.     [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bubble Mix That Has to Rest] **🧰 You need:** 1 litre warm water · 100 ml washing-up liquid · 2 tbsp sugar · 1 lidded jug · 1 long spoon.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bubble Mix That Has to Rest] 2. Sugar in. Stir slow with the spoon, ten turns, and stop. Fast stirring makes foam, and foam kills a big bubble.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Bubble Mix That Has to Rest] 3. Lid on, jug on the windowsill. It sits until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Every Wand Makes a Round Bubble] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**

    [🎨 The Main Event: Every Wand Makes a Round Bubble] 2. A drinking straw each, hers snipped with a small hole near the top so she cannot suck the mix back up. Dip, blow gentle, and let her work until the first bubble comes off.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Every Wand Makes a Round Bubble] 3. Slotted spoon next, then the colander, which makes forty at once and gets done twice. Then the square pipe cleaner and the triangle, and let her call the shape first.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Every Wand Makes a Round Bubble] 4. The sock bottle: dip the sock end, blow through the neck, and a rope of foam comes out.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Every Wand Makes a Round Bubble] 5. The jam jar lid ring for a clean circle. Then the two straws with the string loop, out on the grass: one in each hand, dip the whole loop, walk backwards. That one makes a bubble the size of her.
    [🎨 The Main Event: Every Wand Makes a Round Bubble] 6. Back to the first question. Seven holes, a square and a triangle among them, and every bubble came out round.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Reading the Wind with Bubbles] Take the jug and one straw to the widest bit of the green. Blow a long run of
    [🌳 Get Outside: Reading the Wind with Bubbles] bubbles tight against a wall, then out in the open, and mark the windier spot
    [🌳 Get Outside: Reading the Wind with Bubbles] with a stone.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Chalk Clock] 2. Draw round her shadow in the first colour while she holds still. Arms out makes it easier for you and funnier for her.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** Ice water is five seconds, counted out loud, then the towel. Do not let her leave her hands in it and never hold ice against her skin. Kreston does not go near the bowl. Run the heavy carry along a cleared route with nobody sitting on the floor in it.

    [🎨 The Main Event: Six Hard Things in a Row] 6. Tape the paper to the wall at her height and count the six marks with her.

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Hill You Have to Walk Up] asks to be carried. Stop, count ten breaths together, then keep going on her

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Sponge Pump] 7. Tip both bowls out onto the grass, wipe the tray, and dry her hands and feet on the towels.
D164 (Sun Jun 13) The Lifeguard
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The chair. A dining chair seat puts her head above your chest, and a three-year-old who stands up on one, leans out for a teddy or gets nudged by a brother goes down head first onto whatever is underneath. Nothing goes under the legs to raise it, she sits rather than stands, the chair goes on grass rather than on the paving, and she is never up there while you are indoors or round the side of the house. When you leave the garden, the chair comes with you. Mid-June sun on paving is the other one. The path gets hot enough to hurt bare feet by one o'clock, and a 12-month-old overheats faster than she does, so sun hats on both of them, shade to move into, and the jug of water going round every twenty minutes. Keep Kreston out of direct sun altogether.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Whistle and What It Means] **🧰 You need:** 1 whistle, or one word you both agree to shout · everyone who is in the house.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Whistle and What It Means] 2. Agree what it means: everybody stops and looks at whoever blew it. One whistle, one rule.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: The Whistle and What It Means] 3. Practise on the household. She blows, everyone freezes, including you. Six goes and she owns it.

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Chalk Pool on the Path] where the lifeguard chair stands this afternoon.

D178 (Sun Jun 27) Red, White and Frozen
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A 12-month-old drowns in the depth of water that is in that tub. Kreston never sits beside it unwatched, and you tip it out the moment you go in, not later.     [🌅 Opening Activity: Mashing the Blueberries] **🧰 You need:** the punnet of blueberries · 1 fork · 1 jug.

    [🌅 Opening Activity: Mashing the Blueberries] 2. Stand the jug in the fridge. This is the top layer and it waits until late morning.

    [🎨 The Main Event: The Three-Layer Ice Pops] 4. Tear eight squares of kitchen foil. Press one over each cup and push a lolly stick through the middle into the yoghurt, which holds it standing.

    [🎨 The Main Event: The Three-Layer Ice Pops] 6. At 3:30, run one cup under the warm tap for three seconds and peel the paper off in one piece.

    [🌳 Get Outside: The Coolest Spot on the Street] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tub Under the Sheet] 2. Set the washing-up bowl in that shade and fill it a hand's depth with cold water. No deeper, today or any other day.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tub Under the Sheet] 6. The sponge goes in last: fill, lift, squeeze over her own knees, and half the tub ends up on the grass.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Tub Under the Sheet] 7. Drinking water stands in the shade beside you and both of them drink at the top of every hour. When she has finished, tip the bowl out and turn it over.
D179 (Mon Jun 28) Roll, Then Tell
  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** The permanent marker and its cap go back on the table out of Kreston's reach the moment you stop writing, at 8:00 and again over the book. Everything else on the floor is his: check the cube for loose tape before you roll it to him, and pick the masking tape and the offcuts up as you go. On the corner walk you take the kerb side. The turn is hers and the road is yours, at every corner, including the ones where she has already set off.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on All Six Sides] **🧰 You need:** the small square box · 1 permanent marker · 1 roll masking tape.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on All Six Sides] 1. Tape the box shut on every open edge so it holds its shape when it lands.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on All Six Sides] 2. Ask her for six things: a person, an animal, a place, a weather, a food, and one of her own choosing. Draw each on a face while she watches. Yours will not be good and she will tell you so.
    [🌅 Opening Activity: Drawing on All Six Sides] 3. Roll it across the floor once. Whatever lands on top, name it out loud. Then it sits in the middle of the table until 9:15.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Story the Dice Tells] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Story the Dice Tells] 1. Sit on the floor with the paper and the pen beside you. Write *Once there was* at the top of the first sheet and let her finish the sentence out loud.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Story the Dice Tells] 2. She rolls the cube. Whatever is on top is the next thing that happens in the story, and she says how.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Story the Dice Tells] 3. Write her words down as they come. No tidying, no fixing the grammar, no better version of what she meant.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Story the Dice Tells] 4. Roll again. Six or seven rolls is a whole story, and the same picture landing twice means it comes back.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Story the Dice Tells] 5. If a face has stopped working for her, tape a fresh square of paper over it and draw something new with the permanent marker. She chooses what.
    [🎨 The Main Event: The Story the Dice Tells] 6. Read the finished thing back to her, out loud, at full commitment, in her exact words.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Whatever Happens at the Next Corner] At every corner she calls left or right and you go that way without comment. Between corners, narrate what you pass as if it were the story: the cat on the wall wanted something, the parked van has been there since Friday. Late June mornings are warm by eleven, so take the shady side home.
    [🌳 Get Outside: Whatever Happens at the Next Corner] <div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Six Pages and a Cover] Her story exists on four loose sheets in your handwriting. This afternoon it becomes a book.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Six Pages and a Cover] **🧰 Complete Materials List:**
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Six Pages and a Cover] 1. Fold the four sheets in half, stack them one inside another, and run masking tape down the outside of the fold. That is a cover and six pages, with a couple spare.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Six Pages and a Cover] 2. Read the story back and stop at each thing that happens. One thing, one page. Number them with the permanent marker while she watches.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Six Pages and a Cover] 3. Crayons out. She draws page one, and you say nothing about what it looks like and add nothing to it.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Six Pages and a Cover] 4. Write her sentence for that page underneath the drawing, word for word, the same as this morning.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Six Pages and a Cover] 5. Keep going. Six pages if she stays, three pages if she does not, and three pages is a book.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Six Pages and a Cover] 6. Cover last. She chooses the title and you write it, then she does her own name however her name comes out.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: Six Pages and a Cover] 7. Sit Kreston down and let her read it to him. She will not read the words, and it does not matter.

  SAFETY: ⚠️ **Safety:** A whole day outdoors at the end of June is a heat day. Water at every station and not only when she asks, hats on, and the middle of the day spent in shade.     [🎨 Second Main Event: The Course She Sets Out Herself] 1. Hand her the chalk and two fixed points: the course starts at the back door and ends wherever she says.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Course She Sets Out Herself] 2. She draws the line. Walk behind her and mark nothing yourself, including the bit that goes through the flower bed.

    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Course She Sets Out Herself] 6. The jug of water and the cups live at the middle station. She drinks a full cup every time she passes it, and so does Kreston.
    [🎨 Second Main Event: The Course She Sets Out Herself] 7. She changes the course whenever she likes. A wet foot rubs the chalk out and she redraws it somewhere worse.
